Preface to Book v1.0.0
Book v1.0.0 is the publication baseline for this guide and the canonical starting
  point for all future editions. It treats an agentic application as a governed
  distributed system: models may propose, while deterministic services own
          authority, state, effects, evidence, acceptance, and release.
The narrative follows one dependency chain. Foundations establish the deterministic shell; the reference architecture
assigns ownership; the developer platform makes those controls reusable; patterns and anti-patterns guide topology;
gateways and security enforce boundaries; observability and evaluation produce evidence; continuous learning converts
evidence into governed change; and production operations keep the resulting system reliable and economical.

Scope and claim discipline
External facts, protocol behavior, framework capabilities, research findings, standards, and legal or regulatory context
use numbered citations such as [R05]. Architecture choices without citations are recommendations authored for this
book; they must be validated against the reader’s workload, jurisdiction, threat model, provider terms, and operating
constraints. A source supports only the claim stated beside it—vendor documentation does not establish production
fitness, community guidance is not law, and a benchmark result does not guarantee enterprise outcomes.

Reproducible edition
The accompanying Book v1.0.0 development package contains this canonical Markdown, the generated DOCX, source
files, cover and diagrams, a reference registry, style tokens, templates, skills, build tools, validation tools, and release
evidence. Future agents should modify the canonical Markdown and registries, never hand-format the release DOCX.
The build and validation workflow is documented in the package README.

                                  Figure 0-1 — Narrative map from foundations to production operations


Reference-guide contract
1.   Probabilistic components propose; deterministic components authorize and commit.
2.   Every run is pinned to immutable prompt, Skill, workflow, policy, model-route, tool, evaluator, and knowledge
     versions.
3.   Every side effect has one owner, an idempotency key, an effect record, and executable acceptance.
4.   Every trust-boundary crossing revalidates identity, authority, content, and data policy.
5.   Every material change is evaluated, approved at the right risk tier, canaried, observable, and reversible.

                                         PART I

                         FOUNDATIONS
   This Part defines the vocabulary and invariants used everywhere else. It begins with the
  progression from model call to production system, then turns model outputs into typed tool
proposals, and finally places bounded reasoning behind a framework-neutral runtime contract.
                  Read it before selecting orchestration patterns or products.




                                    BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 8

01 — From an LLM Call to a Production System
  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT Northstar Components wants to shorten supplier onboarding from twelve days to
  two. A prototype can summarize a supplier file, but production must also retrieve authorized records, request missing
  evidence, pause for people, screen sanctions, create auditable decisions, and survive days of inactivity.



  DESIGN DECISION Start Northstar as a governed distributed application with one bounded reasoning component,
  not as an autonomous chatbot.




What this chapter delivers
Explain why an agentic application is a distributed system and assign every authoritative decision to the correct
component.

Solution blueprint
Figure 1. From an LLM Call to a Production System.
State owner                   Trust boundary                Scale unit                       Failure containment          Core contracts

Run Service and durable       User/edge, content/model,                                                                   RunManifest · AgentStep ·
                                                            run · tenant · regional cell     one run and one cell
workflow                      model/tool                                                                                  Artifact




The progression: model, tool, agent, system
A language model maps input tokens to output tokens. A tool-using model can propose structured interaction with the
environment. An agent chooses among steps using observations. An enterprise agentic system adds everything
needed to make those choices safe and operational: durable state, identity, authorization, bounded resources, external-
effect semantics, evidence, human control, deployment compatibility, evaluation, and incident recovery.
This distinction prevents a common category error. Better prompting can improve a reasoning step; it cannot provide
transactional integrity, credential isolation, deterministic replay, tenant boundaries, or a provable audit trail. Those are
properties of the surrounding system.
Uncertainty                                       Appropriate mechanism                                Authoritative owner

Meaning of an ambiguous request                   Model or classifier                                  Bounded agent step

Which known path applies                          Router plus deterministic validation                 Route service / workflow

Unknown task decomposition                        Planner plus plan compiler                           Plan service / workflow

Current business state                            Database read through governed service               Domain system of record

Permission to act                                 RBAC/ABAC and delegated capability                   Policy engine

Whether a write committed                         Effect receipt or reconciliation                     Effect ledger + downstream system

Whether the run is complete                       Executable acceptance criteria                       Workflow / acceptance engine



The Northstar domain thread
Throughout this book, Northstar builds a supplier-onboarding platform. The evolving system retrieves candidate records,
validates documents, screens sanctions, requests corrections, coordinates specialists, waits for human decisions,
creates supplier records, and preserves evidence. The use case is deliberately inconvenient: it crosses data boundaries,
lasts days, includes ambiguous reasoning, performs protected writes, and requires audit. That makes it useful for testing
whether an architecture is real.




                                                             BOOK v1.0.0        |   PAGE 9
                                                               ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Anti-pattern — the autonomous monolith
A single process receives a prompt, holds a broad user token, queries databases, calls arbitrary APIs, stores memory,
retries writes, and declares completion. It is easy to demonstrate and nearly impossible to operate safely. A production
decomposition does not remove intelligence; it gives intelligence explicit contracts and failure boundaries.

The production problem
An enterprise agent must translate an ambiguous goal into actions across systems that have strict identity, consistency,
availability, privacy, cost, and compliance requirements. The LLM is nondeterministic; the environment is concurrent and
partially failing; inputs may be adversarial; and many actions are irreversible. Production design therefore has to answer
three questions for every step:
38. May this principal perform this action on this resource now?
39. Can this step be retried, resumed, canceled, or compensated safely?
40. Can an operator later reconstruct what happened, why, under which versions and permissions?

39            error_code: str | None = None




Debugging playbook
41. Trace one request across model, workflow, tool, human, and record owners.
42. Find any place where prompt text is being treated as authoritative state.
43. Reproduce one partial failure and identify the missing durable fact.

Production checklist
✓ The model is a bounded reasoning component—not the owner of workflow state.
✓ Identity, policy, state, effects, approvals, and acceptance each have an authoritative owner.
✓ Autonomy is bounded independently across tools, data, time, cost, delegation, and publication.
✓ Consequential effects are authorized, deduplicated, verified, and recoverable.
✓ Operators can reconstruct the run from versions, evidence, decisions, and receipts.

Exercises
44. Draw the minimum production architecture for Northstar and label every authoritative store.
45. Convert a chatbot feature list into measurable outcome, authority, duration, data, and effect requirements.

02 — Tools, Structured Outputs, and the
Deterministic Shell
  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT The prototype now proposes a call to create a supplier record. The JSON is valid
  and the rationale sounds convincing, yet the record may be stale, the user may lack write authority, and a retry could
  create a duplicate. Northstar introduces a deterministic shell around every model proposal.



  DESIGN DECISION Treat every model output as a typed proposal; validate, authorize, commit, verify, and accept
  outside the model.




What this chapter delivers
Turn model output into typed proposals and place validation, authorization, effects, and acceptance behind deterministic
boundaries.

Solution blueprint
Figure 2. Tools, Structured Outputs, and the Deterministic Shell.
State owner               Trust boundary                 Scale unit                       Failure containment   Core contracts

Tool Gateway and domain   model proposal to authorized   tool class · connector ·                               ToolManifest · ToolIntent ·
                                                                                          one logical effect
service                   capability                     downstream limit                                       EffectRecord




Structured output is a protocol boundary
Structured output is valuable because it makes a model decision parseable, versionable, and testable. It is not proof. A
valid ToolIntent can still reference an ineligible tool, exceed delegated authority, target stale state, omit required
evidence, or propose a duplicate mutation. The platform therefore follows propose → validate → authorize → commit
→ verify → accept.
A useful proposal is small. It names one disposition, one tool or delegation target, canonical arguments, evidence
references, uncertainty, and the expected resource version. It does not carry credentials, policy truth, or an instruction to
bypass a failing control.

Deterministic acceptance
Acceptance is not the model saying “done.” Northstar declares supplier onboarding complete only when required
evidence artifacts exist, every screening result is fresh, approvals cover the exact proposal, all protected effects are
committed with receipts, no unknown effect remains, and the supplier record satisfies domain invariants.

Scaling the shell
Scale tool work by workload and risk class, not with one global queue. Read-only retrieval can use high concurrency.
Browser sessions, code sandboxes, high-risk writes, and human approvals need independent quotas and backpressure.
The Tool Gateway is stateless where possible; manifests, policy, effect records, and receipts live in durable stores.
Downstream concurrency limits, not model throughput, usually determine safe capacity.

Design principles
46. Deterministic shell, agentic core. Put LLM reasoning inside typed activities; keep authorization, state transitions,
    budgets, validation, approvals, and side-effect commits in code.
47. Least agency. Grant the minimum autonomy, tools, data, time, tokens, fan-out, and mutation scope needed for the
    task.


                                                         BOOK v1.0.0        |   PAGE 13
                                                                    ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



48. One run, one accountable owner. A run may delegate, but exactly one coordinator owns completion, user
    communication, cancellation, and result aggregation.
49. State outside the prompt. Prompts are projections of durable state, not the database. Reconstruct context from
    authoritative stores on each step.
50. Capability by contract. Every agent and tool publishes a typed, versioned manifest; discovery never implies
    authorization.
51. Policy at every boundary. Evaluate authorization at ingress, retrieval, delegation, tool execution, data egress,
    memory write, and artifact publication.
52. At-least-once by default. Assume retries and duplicates. Require idempotency or compensation before a mutating
    capability can be certified.
53. Evidence before autonomy. Autonomy level rises only after offline evals, sandbox simulation, adversarial testing,
    canary evidence, and stable SLOs.
54. Bounded recursion. Cap steps, wall-clock time, spend, tokens, tool calls, delegation depth, parallel branches, and
    repeated failures.
55. Open telemetry and portable state. Use standard trace/event envelopes and keep the authoritative run record
    outside a proprietary agent runtime.

Where distributed systems must remain authoritative

  BOUNDARY RULE: IF AN OPERATION MUST BE CORRECT EVERY TIME, SURVIVE A CRASH, ENFO
  Boundary rule: If an operation must be correct every time, survive a crash, enforce a permission, coordinate
  concurrent writers, or create an auditable effect, it belongs to deterministic infrastructure. The LLM may decide what
  to attempt; deterministic systems decide whether and how it is committed.

                                              idempotency plus reconciliation under at-

                                              dispatch is unknown until proved otherwise.


Evidence anchors: CLOUDEVENTS; OUTBOX. Full URLs, authority, version, and scope appear in Appendix M.

Pattern reference — Tool Use
    PROBLEM The model can identify an action or information need, but enterprise execution requires typed interfaces,
    current authorization, credentials, idempotency, and evidence.



Architecture and flow
The model proposes ToolIntent; the Tool Gateway validates the exact tool version and schema, reauthorizes the action
and resource, mints a last-hop credential, invokes a connector, verifies the result, and records evidence.
56. Expose only tools eligible for the current run node.
57. Generate a minimal typed intent with evidence and uncertainty.
58. Validate schema, policy, risk, budget, approval, and resource version.
59. Invoke through a connector with timeout, idempotency, and egress controls.
60. Return a sanitized result or artifact reference and update durable state.
Use when                                                              Do not use when

Use whenever the agent must read external truth or request an         Do not expose raw SDKs, database handles, credentials, or broad
external effect.                                                      generic tools directly to model-generated code.



Trade-offs
Choice                                                                Consequence

                                                                      More interfaces to maintain; far stronger policy, testing, observability,
Typed narrow tools
                                                                      and least privilege.

                                                                      Smaller prompts and flexible catalogs; requires deterministic eligibility
Dynamic discovery
                                                                      and version resolution.



Failures and controls
Failure                                                               Control

Hallucinated tool or arguments                                        Schema and eligibility rejection.

                                                                      Logical effect key, intent hash, downstream idempotency, and
Duplicate mutation
                                                                      receipt.

                                                                      Connector limits, content scanning, classification, and artifact
Oversized or malicious output
                                                                      indirection.



Minimum observability
     tool and version
     argument and result hashes
     policy decision
     credential lease ID
     latency and retries

Implementation reference
Governed Tool Gateway
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
    PYTHON

1            from __future__ import annotations
2            from dataclasses import dataclass
3            from datetime import datetime, timezone
4            from typing import Awaitable, Callable, Literal
5            from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
6            from .approvals import ActionProposal, ApprovalGrant, verify_approval
7            from .effects import EffectOutcomeUnknown, InMemoryEffectLedger
8
9            class ToolManifest(BaseModel):
10               tool_id:str; version:str; description:str
11               risk_class:int=Field(ge=0,le=4); mutating:bool; required_action:str
12               timeout_seconds:float=Field(gt=0,le=300); approval_required:bool=False
13               idempotency_required:bool=False
14
15           class InvocationContext(BaseModel):
16               tenant_id:str; subject_id:str; actor_id:str; run_id:str; purpose:str
17               allowed_actions:frozenset[str]; eligible_tool_versions:frozenset[str]
18               policy_digest:str="policy:unspecified"; region:str="unspecified"
19
20           class ToolResult(BaseModel):
21               status:Literal["succeeded","declined","unknown"]
22               output:dict[str,object]=Field(default_factory=dict); effect_key:str|None=None
23
24           class ToolGateway:
25               def __init__(self,effect_ledger:InMemoryEffectLedger|None=None)->None:
26                   self._tools:dict[str,RegisteredTool]={}; self._effects=effect_ledger or InMemoryEffectLedger()
27               def register(self,manifest:ToolManifest,connector:Connector)->None:
28                   key=f"{manifest.tool_id}@{manifest.version}"
29                   if key in self._tools: raise ValueError(f"tool already registered: {key}")
30                   if manifest.mutating and not manifest.idempotency_required:
31                       raise ValueError("mutating tools must declare idempotency_required")
32                   self._tools[key]=RegisteredTool(manifest,connector)
33               def discover(self,ctx:InvocationContext)->tuple[ToolManifest,...]:
34                   return tuple(item.manifest for key,item in sorted(self._tools.items()) if key in
             ctx.eligible_tool_versions and item.manifest.required_action in ctx.allowed_actions)
35               async def
             invoke(self,*,ctx:InvocationContext,tool_version:str,arguments:dict[str,object],logical_effect_key:str|
             None=None,proposal:ActionProposal|None=None,approval:ApprovalGrant|None=None,actual_resource_version:int|
             None=None)->ToolResult:
36                   if tool_version not in ctx.eligible_tool_versions: raise PermissionError("tool is not eligible for
             this run node")
37                   item=self._tools.get(tool_version)
38                   if item is None: raise LookupError("eligible tool version is not registered")
39                   m=item.manifest
40                   if m.required_action not in ctx.allowed_actions: raise PermissionError("required action is outside
             delegated authority")
41                   if m.approval_required:
42                       if proposal is None or approval is None or actual_resource_version is None: raise
             PermissionError("proposal-bound approval is required")
43                       if proposal.model_dump(mode="json",exclude={"expires_at"}) !=
             ActionProposal(tenant_id=ctx.tenant_id,run_id=ctx.run_id,actor_id=ctx.actor_id,tool_version=tool_version,log
             ical_effect_key=logical_effect_key or
             "",arguments=arguments,expected_resource_version=proposal.expected_resource_version,policy_digest=ctx.policy
             _digest,expires_at=proposal.expires_at).model_dump(mode="json",exclude={"expires_at"}): raise
             PermissionError("proposal does not match invocation")
44                       verify_approval(proposal,approval,now=datetime.now(timezone.utc),actual_resource_version=actual_
             resource_version)
45                   if not m.mutating:
46                       return ToolResult(status="succeeded",output=await item.connector(arguments,None))
47                   if not logical_effect_key: raise ValueError("mutating tools require a stable logical effect key")
48                   intent={"tool":tool_version,"arguments":arguments,"run_id":ctx.run_id,"tenant_id":ctx.tenant_id}
49                   effect=self._effects.begin(logical_effect_key,intent)
50                   if effect.state=="committed": return ToolResult(status="succeeded",output=effect.receipt or
             {},effect_key=logical_effect_key)




                                                     BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 16
                                                                     ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



51                if effect.state in {"executing","unknown","intervention_required"}: raise
          EffectOutcomeUnknown(logical_effect_key)
52                if effect.state=="declined": return ToolResult(status="declined",output=effect.receipt or
          {},effect_key=logical_effect_key)
53                self._effects.mark_executing(logical_effect_key,f"req:{logical_effect_key}")
54                try: output=await item.connector(arguments,logical_effect_key)
55                except Exception as exc:
56                    self._effects.mark_unknown(logical_effect_key)
57                    raise EffectOutcomeUnknown(logical_effect_key) from exc
58                committed=self._effects.mark_committed(logical_effect_key,output)
59                return ToolResult(status="succeeded",output=committed.receipt or {},effect_key=logical_effect_key)




Debugging playbook
61. Compare the raw model proposal with the validated command and list rejected fields.
62. Inspect the policy decision and resource version immediately before commit.
63. Replay the same logical mutation and verify one receipt.

Production checklist
✓ Every model output is classified as a proposal by default.
✓ State transitions, policy, budgets, business rules, and protected commits sit behind deterministic interfaces.
✓ Proposals are minimal, typed, evidence-linked, and explicit about uncertainty.
✓ Resource versions and obligations are rechecked immediately before commit.
✓ Completion uses deterministic acceptance criteria.

Exercises
64. Define a typed proposal and deterministic acceptance function for supplier screening.
65. Write five invariants that must be true before supplier activation can commit.

Technical review questions
Question                                                                 Strong answer

                                                                         Semantic interpretation, evidence synthesis, and bounded planning
What belongs in the agentic core versus the deterministic shell?         in the core; authority, state, policy, concurrency, effects, and
                                                                         acceptance in the shell.

                                                                         The model emits a typed proposal; deterministic services validate
Explain propose–validate–commit.                                         schema, authority, risk, budget, evidence, and version; only a domain
                                                                         service commits and records a receipt.

                                                                         It reduces parsing ambiguity but does not prove truth, authorization,
Why is structured model output necessary but insufficient?
                                                                         freshness, business validity, idempotency, or safety.

                                                                         In an acceptance engine or workflow guard using required artifacts,
Where should completion be decided?                                      effect states, policy obligations, and domain invariants—not from a
                                                                         model’s “done” message.

                                                                         More contracts and platform complexity, but improved safety,
What is the trade-off of a stronger deterministic shell?                 replayability, testing, provider portability, incident recovery, and
                                                                         auditability.

03 — Agent Runtime and Framework Abstraction
    Evidence note. Concrete adapter examples use OpenAI Agents SDK v0.22.2 and its documented agent, tool,
    guardrail, handoff, result, and tracing surfaces; the platform contracts remain framework-neutral. [R29][R30]
    This chapter uses the OpenAI Agents SDK as a concrete adapter. Enterprise applications should compile platform-
    owned contracts into this or another framework through conformance-tested adapters; Chapters 4–7 define the
    portable architecture.

  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT Northstar adopts OpenAI Agents SDK for its compact agent loop, tools, handoffs,
  structured outputs, tracing, sessions, and human-interruption surfaces. The architecture team refuses to let SDK
  state become workflow truth or to expose raw enterprise credentials to the agent process.



  DESIGN DECISION Place the OpenAI Agents SDK behind AgentRuntime; pin 0.22.2 and keep workflow, authority,
  credentials, effects, and audit platform-owned.




What this chapter delivers
Use OpenAI Agents SDK 0.22.2 as a bounded, replaceable runtime adapter rather than the enterprise control plane.

Solution blueprint
Figure 3. Agent Runtime and OpenAI Agents SDK.
State owner                   Trust boundary             Scale unit                      Failure containment         Core contracts

                                                                                                                     AgentRuntime ·
Durable workflow; SDK only    platform contract to SDK   worker pool · model route ·
                                                                                         one activity attempt        AgentStepRequest ·
owns one bounded step         adapter                    tool class
                                                                                                                     RunState




Framework baseline and placement
This edition pins openai-agents==0.22.2 and openai>=2.25.0 as an implementation baseline. The SDK provides useful
application-level primitives: Agent, Runner, function tools, agents-as-tools, handoffs, guardrails, structured output,
sessions, tracing, MCP integration, deferred tool loading, ToolSearchTool, tool namespaces, human interruption,
serializable RunState, and sandbox-oriented agent surfaces.
Northstar uses those primitives for one bounded agent activity. A durable workflow calls a platform-owned
AgentRuntime; the OpenAI adapter builds the eligible agent and tool set, invokes Runner, and translates the result back
into provider-neutral contracts. This makes the SDK valuable without making it the owner of long-lived business truth.
SDK concern                                                              Northstar placement

Agent instructions and structured output                                 SDK adapter, pinned in release manifest

Turn loop and local handoff                                              SDK worker, bounded by external limits

Sessions / RunState                                                      Optional resumable runtime state; never canonical workflow state

Function tools and MCP                                                   Narrow façades that call the Tool Gateway

Guardrails                                                               Defense in depth; policy still enforced outside the model

Tracing                                                                  Exported and correlated with workflow/effect/audit IDs

Approval interruption                                                    Mapped to durable ApprovalRequest and workflow wait

Credentials and business writes                                          Tool Gateway and domain services only




                                                         BOOK v1.0.0       |   PAGE 18
                                                             ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Upgrade discipline
An SDK upgrade is a behavior change even when public types remain compatible. Northstar runs adapter contract tests,
recorded trajectory replay, tool-schema tests, approval interruption tests, model-route evaluations, load tests, and canary
releases. Active workflows retain the SDK and agent-definition versions in their RunManifest.

Architectural role
The OpenAI Agents SDK is best treated as a bounded agent harness, not the platform kernel. It contributes model-
facing ergonomics—agent definitions, tools, structured outputs, local handoffs, run context, streaming, guardrails, and
tracing hooks. Enterprise durability, delegated authorization, business transactions, approvals, effect reconciliation,
tenant isolation, and audit remain platform responsibilities.

Stable runtime contract
Keep public Run APIs and workflow history independent of SDK types. A platform-owned boundary can be expressed
as:
TEXT · implementation excerpt
 TEXT

1         AgentRuntime.execute(AgentStepRequest) -> AgentStepResult


AgentStepRequest pins run and node identity, state version, agent and model route, authorized context manifest, eligible
tools, capability ceiling, output schema, resource limits, deadline, and trace context. AgentStepResult returns a typed
disposition, immutable artifact references, proposed actions, a concise evidence-grounded summary, usage, trace
references, and a structured error.

One bounded step
66. The durable workflow schedules a reasoning activity with pinned versions and a deadline.
67. Context is materialized from an authorized ContextManifest.
68. The adapter creates SDK agents and only the tool façades eligible for this node.
69. External controls enforce turns, time, tokens, cost, tool calls, parallelism, and delegation depth.
70. The SDK executes the local model/tool loop.
71. The adapter validates the structured result and records usage and trace linkage.
72. Workflow code—not the SDK—chooses the next durable transition.

Bounded Python adapter

19            subject_id: str
20            actor_id: str
21            run_id: str
22            gateway: ToolGateway

23

25      class StepDisposition(BaseModel):
26          status: Literal["completed", "needs_input", "proposed_action"]
27          summary: str
28          evidence_artifact_ids: list[str]
29
30
31      @tool
32      async def retrieve_supplier_policy(
33          ctx: RunContextWrapper[PlatformContext], country: str
34      ) -> dict[str, object]:
35          """Retrieve authorized policy through the enterprise Tool Gateway."""
36          return await ctx.context.gateway.invoke(
37              tenant_id=ctx.context.tenant_id,
38              subject_id=ctx.context.subject_id,
39              actor_id=ctx.context.actor_id,
40              run_id=ctx.context.run_id,
41              tool="supplier.policy.read.v2",
42              arguments={"country": country},
43          )
44

46      agent = Agent[PlatformContext](
47          name="Supplier analyst",
48          model=os.environ["OPENAI_AGENT_MODEL"],
49          instructions=(
50              "Analyze only supplied authorized evidence. Return a structured "
51              "disposition. Never claim that an external write occurred."
52          ),
53          tools=[retrieve_supplier_policy],
54          output_type=StepDisposition,
55      )
56
57
58      async def execute_step(request, context: PlatformContext) -> dict[str, object]:
59          result = await Runner.run(
60              starting_agent=agent,
61              input=request.input_items,
62              context=context,
63              max_turns=request.max_turns,
64              run_config=RunConfig(
65                   workflow_name="supplier-onboarding",
66                   group_id=request.run_id,
67                   trace_include_sensitive_data=False,
68                   trace_metadata={
69                       "run_id": request.run_id,
70                       "agent_version": request.agent_version,
71                   },
72                   tool_execution=ToolExecutionConfig(
73                       max_function_tool_concurrency=2,
74                   ),
75              ),
76          )
77          output = StepDisposition.model_validate(result.final_output)
78          return {
79              "status": output.status,
80              "decision_summary": output.summary,
81              "output_artifacts": output.evidence_artifact_ids,
82              "usage": str(result.context_wrapper.usage),
83          }


The model and SDK never receive a reusable production credential. The tool façade submits identifiers and typed
arguments to the gateway, which reauthorizes the exact resource, mints a short-lived last-hop credential, enforces
effects and quotas, and sanitizes the result.

Handoffs and specialists
Use Agent.as\_tool() when a manager must retain control of the final result. Use an SDK handoff when conversational
control genuinely transfers inside one bounded activity. Use a durable TaskEnvelope and child workflow when the work



                                                 BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 20
                                                                ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



needs independent ownership, retries, waits, deadlines, scaling, cancellation, or a cross-service trust boundary. A
conversational handoff must never silently transfer budget, authority, task ownership, or cancellation responsibility.

Guardrails, state, and retries
SDK guardrails are valuable for early input, tool, and output checks. They do not replace canonical schema validation, a
policy decision point, Tool Gateway authorization, or domain transaction rules. SDK sessions can assist local continuity,
but run state, plan revisions, budgets, approvals, effects, policy decisions, and compliance evidence remain authoritative
elsewhere.
Assign one retry owner per failure class: the workflow owns activity retries; the Model Gateway may perform short
transport retries inside one recorded attempt; the Tool Gateway follows the tool manifest and effect protocol; an unknown
mutation is reconciled before any retry.

Adoption test
Adopt the SDK only after proving that tools cannot bypass gateways, every invocation is externally bounded, handoffs
map to durable ownership correctly, sensitive tracing is disabled or isolated, protected commits require deterministic
validation, and another runtime could replace the adapter without migrating public APIs, workflow history, effects,
artifacts, approvals, or audit.

51   def serialize_interrupted_run(result:Any)->str:
52       if not result.interruptions: raise ValueError("run has no pending approval")
53       # Custom PlatformContext contains a live gateway; configure an application serializer
54       # or persist identifiers separately and rebuild context when restoring the snapshot.
55       return result.to_state().to_string()
56
57   async def approve_in_memory_and_resume(result:Any,interruption_index:int=0):
58       """Minimal SDK flow; production first verifies the enterprise ApprovalGrant."""
59       state=result.to_state(); state.approve(result.interruptions[interruption_index])
60       return await Runner.run(build_supplier_agent(),state)

Debugging playbook
73. Capture the platform request and SDK adapter request and diff their responsibilities.
74. Verify which SDK state is persisted and whether its version is pinned.
75. Disable one tool at the gateway and confirm the agent cannot recover ambient access.

Production checklist
✓ The SDK sits behind a platform-owned AgentRuntime adapter.
✓ Only governed tool façades enter the agent process.
✓ Turns, time, tokens, cost, tool calls, and delegation have external limits.
✓ Local handoffs are distinct from durable task ownership.
✓ Retry, state, trace privacy, and release compatibility are coordinated outside the SDK.

04 — Enterprise Agentic AI Reference Architecture
Purpose and principles
The enterprise architecture places probabilistic reasoning inside explicit experience, runtime, workflow, knowledge,
integration, security, evaluation, and operations planes. The agent is a decision component—not the workflow,
authorization service, database, policy engine, or proof of completion.

End-to-end responsibility flow




                                   Figure 4-2 — Request-to-verified-outcome sequence
78. Authenticate the initiating subject and workload; establish tenant, purpose, risk, deadline, and trace.
79. Admit a run and pin all behavior, policy, data, evaluator, and dependency versions.
80. Build an authorized ContextManifest; preserve provenance and untrusted-content labels.
81. Let the model propose a typed decision. Policy determines eligible models, tools, data, and actions.
82. Bind protected changes to an ActionProposal and exact, expiring ApprovalGrant.
83. Commit through a tool façade with short-lived credentials, idempotency, EffectRecord, and reconciliation.
84. Determine accepted, blocked, needs-input, failed, or unknown-effect using executable business criteria.
85. Join traces, evaluations, human feedback, cost, latency, incidents, and outcomes for controlled improvement.

                                                                                                   terminal

Architecture decisions
Centralize identity, policy, gateways, tool/effect contracts, telemetry schemas, evaluation infrastructure, release
evidence, and incident controls. Federate domain prompts, accepted outcomes, tools, Skills, knowledge, datasets, and
SLOs to accountable owners. Standardize interfaces and evidence—not every framework implementation.

05 — Platform Boundaries, APIs, and Data Models
    Evidence note. CloudEvents defines the portable event envelope used here, while the transactional-outbox source
    supports atomic state/event publication and warns that consumers still require duplicate handling. [R03][R04]

  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT The design now spans edge APIs, definitions, workflows, workers, model routing,
  tools, knowledge, trust, and operations. Northstar turns diagrams into explicit contracts, stores, ownership, partitions,
  and failure domains.



  DESIGN DECISION Deploy global definitions and governance into regional execution cells connected through
  stable provider-neutral contracts.




What this chapter delivers
Translate principles into planes, services, contracts, stores, consistency boundaries, and regional cells.

Solution blueprint
Figure 20. Enterprise Platform Architecture, APIs, and Data Models.
State owner                  Trust boundary                 Scale unit                          Failure containment           Core contracts

Each platform service owns   global control plane to                                                                          Run · Task · Tool · Artifact ·
                                                            tenant · queue · partition · cell   one service/cell
one authoritative record     regional execution cell                                                                          Release APIs




From boxes to contracts
A reference architecture becomes implementable when every arrow has an API or event contract and every record has
one authoritative writer. Northstar separates a global definition and governance plane from regional execution cells. Cells
contain run control, durable workflow, bounded agent workers, model and Tool Gateways, context services, effect
ledger, artifact store, and regional telemetry/audit pipelines.
The public Run API is asynchronous and idempotent. Commands carry expected state version and deadline; accepted
work returns 202 plus an operation or run resource. Status and events are durable and paginated. Large inputs and
outputs use immutable artifact references rather than oversized JSON. Public contracts do not expose SDK messages or
provider-specific run objects.

Core authoritative records
RunManifest, WorkflowExecution, TaskEnvelope, AgentStep, ContextManifest, CapabilityEnvelope, PolicyDecision,
CredentialLease, ToolManifest, ApprovalRequest, EffectRecord, Artifact, EvaluationReport, and ReleaseManifest each
have one owner, version, retention policy, and consistency boundary.
Scale stateless APIs horizontally, partition state by tenant and run, shard workflow history and trace indexes, and isolate
workload classes through queues and concurrency pools. Cell placement enforces region and residency while bounding
blast radius.

Artifact contract
Every artifact has artifact\_id, immutable version, schema/media type, content hash, creator run/node/agent, source
references and hashes, tenant, owner, classification, residency, ACL/purpose policy, confidence/quality metadata,
retention/deletion linkage, and optional signature. Large artifacts move by reference; callers receive short-lived
authorized URLs rather than embedded payloads.

Effect receipt
A mutating tool returns a structured receipt: logical effect ID, idempotency key, downstream request ID, target resource
and before/after version, committed/declined/unknown state, timestamp, actor/subject, compensation reference,
verification query, and audit link. A timeout without a receipt is unknown, not failed.

8            "dataschema": "schema://run-status-changed@1",

A vector database is an index, not the source of truth; an agent transcript is not workflow state; a trace store is not the
audit ledger; and a queue is not a business transaction. Keeping these responsibilities separate prevents attractive
shortcuts from becoming systemic risks.

Grounding, corrections, and evidence boundary
Claim or correction                         Grounded statement                                Source IDs

                                            Use a CloudEvents 1.0-compatible envelope
                                            for interoperability, but keep domain
                                            semantics and delivery guarantees in
Event contract                              versioned event contracts. Source plus ID         CLOUDEVENTS
                                            supports deduplication; large or sensitive
                                            artifacts should be referenced rather than
                                            embedded.


Evidence anchors: CLOUDEVENTS. Full URLs, authority, version, and scope appear in Appendix M.

CloudEvents and outbox contracts
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
PYTHON

1        from __future__ import annotations




                                                 BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 32
                                                             ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



2
3       from datetime import datetime
4       from typing import Any, Literal
5
6       from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator
7
8
9       class CloudEvent(BaseModel):
10          """CloudEvents 1.0-compatible application envelope."""
11
12          specversion: Literal["1.0"] = "1.0"
13          id: str = Field(min_length=1)
14          source: str = Field(min_length=1)
15          type: str = Field(min_length=1)
16          subject: str | None = None
17          time: datetime
18          datacontenttype: str = "application/json"
19          dataschema: str | None = None
20          data: dict[str, Any]
21          tenant_id: str
22          traceparent: str | None = None
23
24          @field_validator("source")
25          @classmethod
26          def source_is_uri_reference(cls, value: str) -> str:
27              if ":" not in value and not value.startswith("/"):
28                  raise ValueError("source must be a URI-reference")
29              return value
30
31
32      class OutboxRecord(BaseModel):
33          event: CloudEvent
34          aggregate_id: str
35          aggregate_version: int = Field(ge=0)
36          state: Literal["pending", "published", "dead_letter"] = "pending"
37          publish_attempts: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
38
39
40      def deduplication_key(event: CloudEvent) -> tuple[str, str]:
41          """CloudEvents producers make (source, id) unique for each distinct event."""
42
43          return event.source, event.id




Debugging playbook
86. Trace one API command through stores, queues, services, and contracts.
87. Find any record with two authoritative writers.
88. Map a cell failure to its intended containment boundary.

Production checklist
✓ Every record and transition has one authoritative owner.
✓ Public contracts remain independent of model providers and SDK types.
✓ Every trust boundary states authentication, authorization, validation, and data handling.
✓ Regional execution can continue safely on pinned releases during control-plane disruption.
✓ Cells, queues, and local dependencies bound failure domains.

Exercises
89. Define Run, Task, Tool, Approval, Effect, Artifact, and Release contracts.
90. Select a store and consistency model for each authoritative record.

Technical review questions
Question                                                                 Strong answer

                                                                         Experience and edge, global definition, run control, durable
Walk through the major planes of an enterprise agent platform.           execution, agent and model, capability, data and context, trust, and
                                                                         operations—with explicit ownership and interfaces.

                                                                         They have different scaling, availability, data-residency, and failure
Why separate the control plane from the execution plane?                 characteristics; immutable definitions can fan out while active tenant
                                                                         runs stay regional.

                                                                         User-to-edge, knowledge-to-model, model-to-workflow, workflow-to-
Where are the most important trust boundaries?                           tool, sandbox-to-network, peer-to-platform, and platform-to-model
                                                                         provider.

                                                                         Additive scale, tenant placement, residency, bounded blast radius,
Why use cell-based deployment?
                                                                         independent canaries, and predictable stateful-service limits.

                                                                         Continue compatible in-flight work on pinned last-known-good
How should execution behave during a control-plane outage?               bundles, apply local denies and kill switches, and restrict high-risk
                                                                         admission when freshness cannot be established.

06 — Agentic Developer Platform and SDK
     Evidence note. The portability layer is informed by current public agent abstractions from OpenAI, Microsoft, and
     Google; capability parity is not assumed and must be proven by adapter conformance tests. [R29][R31][R32]

Platform promise
The developer platform is a reusable paved road that lets teams build differentiated agents without repeatedly rebuilding
identity, policy, tool safety, tracing, evaluation, durable execution, and delivery infrastructure. It is not a mandatory
monolith: applications use platform-owned contracts and replaceable adapters.

Portable contracts and multi-framework support




                        Figure 6-2 — Platform contracts with replaceable framework and provider adapters
Use AgentSpec, RunContext, TaskEnvelope, ToolSpec/ToolCall, ContextManifest, MemoryRecord, EvaluationResult,
and ReleaseManifest as stable contracts. Compile them into framework/provider adapters. Standardized contracts, MCP,
APIs, events, agent-to-agent protocols, gateways, and shared platform services provide interoperability without leaking
vendor-native message objects into applications. Compatibility suites verify cancellation, tools, streaming, approvals,
errors, telemetry, usage, and version behavior.




                                                     BOOK v1.0.0     |   PAGE 37
                                                             ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Developer lifecycle




                                       Figure 6-3 — Local-to-production agent lifecycle
Create from an approved template; attach tools, Skills, context, and memory; configure model policy, permissions,
budgets, and review; test deterministic logic, contracts, simulations, adversarial cases, and failures; evaluate quality,
safety, latency, and cost; package immutable artifacts with locks, SBOM, signature, provenance, and ReleaseManifest;
promote through policy gates; observe correlated production traces; improve through controlled ChangeBundles.

SDK example
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
 PYTHON

1         agent = platform.agent(
2             spec="supplier_onboarding@3.2.0",
3             model_policy="quality-balanced@7",
4             permissions=["supplier:read", "screening:propose"],
5             budgets={"tokens": 18_000, "usd": 0.45, "deadline_s": 90},
6         )
7         agent.attach_skill("supplier-screening@2.4.1")
8         agent.attach_tool(screen_supplier, risk="protected", effect="screening.create")
9         agent.use_context(source="supplier-policy", snapshot="approved-latest")
10        agent.use_memory(policy="tenant-user-preferences@3")
11        report = platform.test(agent).simulate("supplier-onboarding-regression")
12        platform.release(agent, require=["quality", "security", "cost", "rollback"])


The fluent API is convenience. The compiled contracts, policy decisions, tool/effect evidence, traces, and release
manifest are the stable interface.

Testing and simulation pyramid




                                       Figure 7-2 — Testing and simulation pyramid
Deterministic units verify schemas, policy helpers, acceptance, routes, reducers, calculations, and state transitions.
Contract tests verify identity, idempotency, timeout/unknown-effect semantics, protocol capability, errors, and telemetry.
Component evaluations measure retrieval, tools, arguments, structured output, safety, and memory. Trajectory
simulation tests planning, handoffs, loops, deadlines, cancellation, duplicates, dependency degradation, and human
delay. End-to-end staging uses exact model, identity, gateway, data, and integration versions. Canary and shadow
evaluation use explicit stop and rollback conditions.

CLI and delivery workflow
BASH · implementation excerpt
 BASH

1       agentctl init supplier-onboarding --template regulated-workflow --risk high
2       agentctl dev --scenario late-sanctions-response --trace
3       agentctl test --unit --contracts --failure-injection
4       agentctl eval --dataset supplier-regression@12 --compare production
5       agentctl package --lock --sbom --sign
6       agentctl promote --from staging --to production --canary 5%
7       agentctl traces diff run:failed run:accepted
8       agentctl rollback supplier-onboarding@3.2.0 --to 3.1.4


Use one immutable artifact across environments; bind endpoints, identities, secrets, sources, quotas, regions, and
review queues at deployment. Pin SDKs, adapters, model aliases, tool schemas, Skills, MCP servers, policy bundles,
evaluators, and knowledge snapshots. A model/provider swap is a behavior change—not harmless configuration.

Troubleshooting
Start from accepted business outcome and walk backward through the causal run graph. Find the first divergent span:
input classification, source/freshness, prompt, route, model, tool, arguments, policy, effect, approval, or acceptance. Do
not “fix the prompt” until evidence locates the failing responsibility.

lifecycle controls.

08 — Skills as Versioned Capabilities
    NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT Teams begin copying prompts and tool wrappers between agents. Results diverge,
    fixes do not propagate, and no owner can prove which behavior reached production. Northstar introduces a Skill as
    the unit of reusable capability.



    DESIGN DECISION Make the Skill manifest the versioned capability contract and resolve exact releases through
    policy and evaluation evidence.




What this chapter delivers
Define Skills as governed packages of instructions, tools, knowledge, policy, workflows, tests, and operations metadata.

Solution blueprint
Figure 10. Skills as Versioned Capabilities.
State owner                   Trust boundary                  Scale unit                          Failure containment   Core contracts

                                                                                                                        SkillManifest ·
                              semantic discovery to policy-
Skill Registry and resolver                                   skill family · registry partition   one skill version     SkillRequirement ·
                              bound resolution
                                                                                                                        SkillRelease




Skill anatomy
A Skill is the smallest independently owned and versioned package that lets an agent perform a meaningful capability. Its
manifest names interface schemas, instructions, eligible tools, knowledge sources, optional workflow template, policy
constraints, side-effect class, observability fields, evaluation suite, owner, lifecycle, and immutable digest.
Skills separate reuse from copy-and-paste. An application requests a capability such as supplier.evidence.read; it does
not embed a specific prompt and connector. Discovery searches a catalog. Resolution then filters by tenant, purpose,
subject and actor grants, data class, region, risk, compatibility, release stage, health, and cost before pinning one exact
version.

Skill lifecycle
Draft → Certified → Staged → Active → Deprecated → Revoked is evidence-driven. Certification includes contract, unit,
simulation, trajectory, threat, failure-injection, load, and operator-readiness checks. Deprecation retains a compatibility
window for active runs. Revocation removes the Skill from new resolution immediately and may trigger workflow pause
or migration by risk class.

Skill contract surface
     describe(requirement) -> candidates
     resolve(requirement, run\_envelope) -> SkillRelease
     invoke(step\_request) -> AgentStepResult
     evaluate(release, dataset\_version) -> EvaluationReport
     promote(release, stage, evidence) -> ReleaseDecision
The registry scales as a read-heavy control-plane service with immutable manifests, version indexes, cached eligibility
metadata, and regional last-known-good replicas. Runtime execution remains in regional cells.

Implementation reference
Skill manifest and resolver
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
 PYTHON

1         from __future__ import annotations
2
3         import hashlib
4         import json
5         import re
6         from typing import Literal
7
8         from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator, model_validator
9
10        from .contracts import SkillRequirement
11
12
13        _SEMVER = re.compile(
14            r"^(0|[1-9]\d*)\.(0|[1-9]\d*)\.(0|[1-9]\d*)"
15            r"(?:-([0-9A-Za-z-]+(?:\.[0-9A-Za-z-]+)*))?"
16            r"(?:\+[0-9A-Za-z-]+(?:\.[0-9A-Za-z-]+)*)?$"
17        )
18
19
20        def _semver_key(version: str) -> tuple[object, ...]:
21            """Return a SemVer 2.0 precedence key; build metadata has no precedence."""
22
23            match = _SEMVER.fullmatch(version)
24            if match is None:
25                raise ValueError(f"invalid semantic version: {version}")
26            major, minor, patch = (int(match.group(i)) for i in range(1, 4))
27            prerelease = match.group(4)
28            if prerelease is None:
29                return major, minor, patch, 1, ()
30            identifiers: list[tuple[int, int | str]] = []
31            for identifier in prerelease.split("."):
32                if identifier.isdigit():
33                    if len(identifier) > 1 and identifier.startswith("0"):
34                        raise ValueError("numeric prerelease identifiers cannot have leading zeroes")
35                    identifiers.append((0, int(identifier)))
36                else:
37                    identifiers.append((1, identifier))
38            return major, minor, patch, 0, tuple(identifiers)
39
40
41        class SkillManifest(BaseModel):
42            skill_id: str
43            version: str
44            description: str
45            owner: str
46            capabilities: frozenset[str]
47            tool_versions: tuple[str, ...]
48            instruction_ref: str
49            knowledge_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()
50            workflow_ref: str | None = None



                                                     BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 43
                                                           ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



51        allowed_purposes: frozenset[str]
52        allowed_data_classes: frozenset[str]
53        allowed_regions: frozenset[str]
54        maximum_risk: int = Field(ge=0, le=4)
55        status: Literal["draft", "active", "deprecated", "revoked"] = "draft"
56        digest: str | None = None
57
58        @field_validator("version")
59        @classmethod
60        def version_is_semver(cls, value: str) -> str:
61            _semver_key(value)
62            return value
63
64        @model_validator(mode="after")
65        def validate_digest(self) -> "SkillManifest":
66            body = self.model_dump(mode="json", exclude={"digest"})
67            expected = "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(
68                json.dumps(body, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")).encode()
69            ).hexdigest()
70            if self.digest is not None and self.digest != expected:
71                raise ValueError("skill digest mismatch")
72            object.__setattr__(self, "digest", expected)
73            return self
74
75
76    class SkillPackManifest(BaseModel):
77        pack_id: str
78        version: str
79        domain: str
80        owner: str
81        skill_versions: tuple[str, ...]
82        policy_bundle_ref: str
83        evaluation_suite_ref: str
84        status: Literal["draft", "active", "deprecated", "revoked"] = "draft"
85
86        @field_validator("version")
87        @classmethod
88        def version_is_semver(cls, value: str) -> str:
89            _semver_key(value)
90            return value
91
92
93    class SkillRegistry:
94        def __init__(self) -> None:
95            self._skills: dict[str, SkillManifest] = {}
96            self._packs: dict[str, SkillPackManifest] = {}
97
98        def register_skill(self, skill: SkillManifest) -> str:
99            key = f"{skill.skill_id}@{skill.version}"
100           if key in self._skills:
101               raise ValueError(f"skill already exists: {key}")
102           self._skills[key] = skill
103           return key
104
105       def register_pack(self, pack: SkillPackManifest) -> str:
106           key = f"{pack.pack_id}@{pack.version}"
107           if key in self._packs:
108               raise ValueError(f"skill pack already exists: {key}")
109           missing = set(pack.skill_versions) - self._skills.keys()
110           if missing:
111               raise ValueError(f"pack references unknown skills: {sorted(missing)}")
112           if pack.status == "active" and any(
113               self._skills[skill_key].status != "active"
114               for skill_key in pack.skill_versions
115           ):
116               raise ValueError("active pack must reference only active skills")
117           self._packs[key] = pack
118           return key
119
120       def resolve(self, requirement: SkillRequirement) -> SkillManifest:
121           candidates = [
122               skill
123               for skill in self._skills.values()



                                              BOOK v1.0.0    |   PAGE 44
                                                               ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



124                     if skill.status == "active"
125                     and requirement.capability in skill.capabilities
126                     and requirement.purpose in skill.allowed_purposes
127                     and requirement.region in skill.allowed_regions
128                     and requirement.maximum_risk <= skill.maximum_risk
129                     and requirement.required_data_classes <= skill.allowed_data_classes
130                ]
131                if not candidates:
132                    raise LookupError("no eligible skill")
133                # Production resolution also uses evaluation, health, cost, and rollout policy.
134                return max(candidates, key=lambda skill: _semver_key(skill.version))
135
136           def resolve_pack(
137               self, pack_key: str, *, allow_deprecated_for_replay: bool = False
138           ) -> tuple[SkillManifest, ...]:
139               try:
140                   pack = self._packs[pack_key]
141               except KeyError as error:
142                    raise LookupError("unknown skill pack") from error
143               permitted_pack_states = {"active"}
144               permitted_skill_states = {"active"}
145               if allow_deprecated_for_replay:
146                   permitted_pack_states.add("deprecated")
147                   permitted_skill_states.add("deprecated")
148               if pack.status not in permitted_pack_states:
149                    raise LookupError("skill pack is not available for this resolution mode")
150               skills = tuple(self._skills[key] for key in pack.skill_versions)
151               if any(skill.status not in permitted_skill_states for skill in skills):
152                    raise LookupError("skill pack contains unavailable skill")
153               return skills

09 — Skill Packs and Domain Platforms
    NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT Procurement needs a complete domain experience, not isolated Skills. Evidence
    retrieval, document validation, sanctions screening, policy explanation, and approval preparation must ship as a
    compatible, evaluated pack.



    DESIGN DECISION Ship domain behavior as immutable Skill Packs with compatibility, policy, evaluation, and
    promotion metadata.




What this chapter delivers
Compose compatible Skills into certified, distributable domain capability releases.

Solution blueprint
Figure 11. Skill Packs and Domain Platforms.
State owner               Trust boundary          Scale unit                     Failure containment   Core contracts

                                                                                                       SkillPackManifest ·
Skill Pack Registry and   pack authoring to       domain pack · tenant rollout
                                                                                 one pack release      CompatibilityReport ·
release pipeline          tenant/runtime use      cohort
                                                                                                       EvaluationSuite




From individual capability to domain platform
A Skill Pack composes a coherent domain experience. Northstar’s supplier-onboarding pack pins evidence retrieval,
document validation, sanctions screening, policy explanation, approval preparation, and supplier-record creation Skills. It
also pins shared policy, taxonomy, knowledge strategy, workflow templates, evaluation datasets, dashboards, and
runbooks.
A pack is not a directory of prompts. The pack manifest declares dependency constraints, schema compatibility, required
runtime capabilities, data and region policy, risk ceiling, evaluation gates, release aliases, owner, and support window. A
pack can expose a smaller capability surface to each agent even though the complete domain distribution contains more
Skills.

Pack governance and sharing
Packs move through signed releases and aliases such as development, staging, and production. Tenant rollout policy
can pin a version or cohort. Sharing across business units requires explicit policy and data-boundary review; installing a
pack never grants access to its data sources or tools.

Domain pack examples
     Supplier onboarding: evidence, screening, tax, policy, approval, ERP write.
     Customer support: account read, entitlement, troubleshooting, refund proposal, escalation.
     Software delivery: repository context, test planning, code change, review, deployment evidence.
     Financial close: reconciliation, variance explanation, exception routing, controlled posting.

10 — ReAct: Bounded Reason–Act–Observe Loops
     Evidence note. ReAct was introduced as a prompting paradigm that interleaves reasoning and action; its reported
     benchmark results motivate the loop, not an assertion that unrestricted production autonomy is safe. [R01]

  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT An analyst asks why a supplier is blocked. One retrieval is insufficient: the system
  may need the profile, a screening artifact, and a policy explanation. A bounded ReAct loop is appropriate because
  the next information need depends on the previous observation.



  DESIGN DECISION Use ReAct only for information-dependent next steps and impose external turn, tool, time, cost,
  and no-progress limits.




What this chapter delivers
Implement ReAct with external budgets, governed tools, no-progress detection, and deterministic acceptance.

Solution blueprint
Figure 4. ReAct: Bounded Reason–Act–Observe Loops.
State owner                    Trust boundary          Scale unit                        Failure containment   Core contracts

External loop controller and   observation/model and                                                           AgentDecision · Observation ·
                                                       turn · tool permit · run budget   one bounded loop
acceptance engine              model/tool                                                                      ReActLimits




Why ReAct is useful—and expensive
ReAct interleaves reasoning, action, and observation. Its value is conditional adaptivity: after reading the supplier profile,
the agent may decide that a regional registry lookup is unnecessary, or that a contradictory address requires another
source. The pattern becomes harmful when it substitutes an unconstrained loop for a known process.
Northstar stores only typed decisions, tool intents, observations, evidence references, and stop reasons—not private
chain-of-thought. The external controller maintains counters and normalized signatures. If an agent repeats the same
tool and canonical arguments against the same source revision, or if accepted evidence does not change across turns,
the controller records no progress and terminates or escalates.

Acceptance and failure semantics
A ReAct activity can return answer, needs\_input, delegate, proposed\_action, budget\_exhausted, or failed. A mutating
action never executes inside the unconstrained loop; it becomes a proposal for deterministic policy, approval, effect, and
workflow processing. If a read tool times out, bounded retry may be safe. If a mutation times out, the loop stops because
the effect is unknown.

Capacity model
Plan capacity in turns per successful outcome, not only requests per second. Track model tokens, tool calls, connector
time, observation bytes, and repeated-action rate. Per-tenant permits and hard per-run limits prevent one pathological
loop from consuming a worker pool.

Grounding, corrections, and evidence boundary
  EVIDENCE BOUNDARY This chapter presents an architectural recommendation, not a protocol mandate. Validate
  the pattern against the workload, threat model, evaluation set, and strongest simpler baseline.


Evidence anchors: REACT; NIST-GAI. Full URLs, authority, version, and scope appear in Appendix M.



                                                       BOOK v1.0.0        |   PAGE 51
                                                                      ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Pattern reference — ReAct
    PROBLEM The system cannot know every information-gathering step in advance because the next action depends
    on the previous observation.



Architecture and flow
A bounded controller alternates model decision and governed tool invocation. The controller—not the model—owns
turns, budgets, state, repeated-action detection, and acceptance.
101.         Load goal, authorized context, budgets, and acceptance criteria.
102.         Ask for one typed action or terminal disposition.
103.         Validate and execute the action at the Tool Gateway.
104.         Sanitize and append the observation.
105.         Accept, continue, request input, or terminate on an external rule.
Use when                                                                  Do not use when

Use for exploratory retrieval, diagnosis, or research with a small        Avoid for known workflows, single retrievals, protected commits, or
eligible tool set and measurable stop conditions.                         open-ended browsing without meaningful limits.



Trade-offs
Choice                                                                    Consequence

                                                                          Handles unknown paths; increases latency, cost, and trajectory
Adaptivity
                                                                          variance.

Intermediate observations                                                 Improves grounding; increases attack surface and context pressure.



Failures and controls
Failure                                                                   Control

Tool ping-pong                                                            Normalized repeat signatures and no-progress threshold.

Context poisoning                                                         Treat observations as untrusted, provenance-labeled data.

Budget exhaustion                                                         External turn, call, token, time, and cost enforcement.



Minimum observability
      turn index
      decision type
      eligible tools
      action-observation hash
      information gain
      remaining budgets
      stop reason

Implementation reference
Bounded ReAct controller
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
    PYTHON

1            from __future__ import annotations
2            import hashlib
3            import json
4            from dataclasses import dataclass
5            from typing import Awaitable, Callable, Protocol



                                                            BOOK v1.0.0    |   PAGE 52
                                                        ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



6    from .contracts import AgentDecision
7
8    class ReActLimits:
9        maximum_turns: int = 6
10       maximum_tool_calls: int = 4
11       maximum_identical_observations: int = 2
12
13       def __post_init__(self) -> None:
14           if min(
15               self.maximum_turns,
16               self.maximum_tool_calls,
17               self.maximum_identical_observations,
18           ) < 1:
19               raise ValueError("all ReAct limits must be positive")
20
21   class ReActEngine:
22       """A bounded reason-act-observe loop with an external tool boundary."""
23
24       def __init__(
25           self,
26           model: DecisionModel,
27           invoke_tool: ToolInvoker,
28           limits: ReActLimits = ReActLimits(),
29       ) -> None:
30           self.model = model
31           self.invoke_tool = invoke_tool
32           self.limits = limits
33
34       @staticmethod
35       def _observation_fingerprint(
36           tool_id: str, arguments: dict[str, object], result: dict[str, object]
37       ) -> str:
38           payload = json.dumps(
39               {"tool_id": tool_id, "arguments": arguments, "result": result},
40               sort_keys=True,
41               separators=(",", ":"),
42               default=repr,
43           ).encode()
44           return hashlib.sha256(payload).hexdigest()
45
46       async def run(self, goal: str) -> AgentDecision:
47           observations: list[dict[str, object]] = []
48           identical_observations: dict[str, int] = {}
49           tool_calls = 0
50           for _turn in range(self.limits.maximum_turns):
51               decision = await self.model.decide(
52                   goal=goal, observations=tuple(observations)
53               )
54               if decision.disposition in {"answer", "needs_input", "delegate"}:
55                    return decision
56               if decision.tool_call is None:
57                    raise ValueError("invoke_tool decision is missing tool_call")
58               tool_calls += 1
59               if tool_calls > self.limits.maximum_tool_calls:
60                    raise RuntimeError("tool-call budget exceeded")
61               result = await self.invoke_tool(
62                   decision.tool_call.tool_id,
63                   decision.tool_call.arguments,
64                   decision.tool_call.logical_effect_key,
65               )
66               observations.append(
67                   {"tool": decision.tool_call.tool_id, "result": result}
68               )
69               fingerprint = self._observation_fingerprint(
70                   decision.tool_call.tool_id,
71                   decision.tool_call.arguments,
72                   result,
73               )
74               identical_observations[fingerprint] = (
75                   identical_observations.get(fingerprint, 0) + 1
76               )
77               if (
78                   identical_observations[fingerprint]



                                              BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 53
                                                                ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



79                          >= self.limits.maximum_identical_observations
80                     ):
81                         raise RuntimeError("no-progress loop detected")
82                 raise RuntimeError("ReAct turn limit exceeded")




Debugging playbook
106.       List every turn with normalized action, observation, token, time, and cost deltas.
107.       Identify the first repeated action-observation signature.
108.       Check whether the stop decision came from acceptance criteria or model wording.

Production checklist
✓ The information need truly requires iterative observation.
✓ The controller owns limits, state, tool invocation, and acceptance.
✓ Observations are typed, sanitized, versioned, and provenance-linked.
✓ Repeat-action and no-progress signatures are detected.
✓ Unknown effects or budget exhaustion terminate safely.

Technical review questions
Question                                                            Strong answer

                                                                    When the next information need genuinely depends on a verified
When is ReAct the right pattern?
                                                                    prior observation and the loop can be bounded.

                                                                    It owns budgets, tool eligibility, invocation, state, no-progress
Why must the controller sit outside the model?
                                                                    detection, and terminal transitions.

                                                                    Normalize action-observation signatures, count repeats, evaluate
How do you stop a ReAct loop that keeps rephrasing the same
                                                                    information gain, and terminate or escalate at a deterministic
request?
                                                                    threshold.

                                                                    A typed, sanitized result or artifact reference with provenance,
What should an observation contain?
                                                                    version, classification, and explicit error semantics.

                                                                    For a known workflow, a single retrieval, a protected transaction, or a
When should ReAct not be used?
                                                                    task whose acceptance can be computed directly.

A bounded reason–act loop handles local uncertainty; Chapter 11 adds routing and handoff when known paths or
