11 — Routing, Triage, and Handoffs
    NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT Some requests are policy questions, others are document defects, screening
    disputes, or protected onboarding actions. Northstar needs routing, but it also needs to know when responsibility
    actually transfers and who owns cancellation, deadlines, and the final response.



    DESIGN DECISION Use deterministic route eligibility and fallback; record handoff as an explicit ownership
    transition.




What this chapter delivers
Distinguish route selection from ownership transfer and design confidence, fallback, and authority semantics.

Solution blueprint
Figure 5. Routing, Triage, and Handoffs.
State owner                  Trust boundary                    Scale unit                       Failure containment    Core contracts

Route service and workflow   classifier to privileged route;                                                           RouteDecision · Handoff ·
                                                               route class · specialist queue   one route or handoff
ownership record             owner transfer                                                                            CapabilityEnvelope




Selection is not authority
A router reduces heterogeneity by selecting an implementation: policy explanation, document validation, sanctions
review, or human triage. The model may classify semantic intent, but the eligible set is assembled first from purpose,
authority, risk, data region, release status, service health, and capacity. A route cannot grant a capability that was absent
from the run envelope.
A handoff is a different event. It changes the current owner of an interaction or task. Northstar records old owner, new
owner, state version, unresolved obligations, artifact references, child capability envelope, deadline, and cancellation
lineage. The recipient acknowledges or rejects the transfer. Until acknowledgment, the previous owner remains
accountable.

Confidence and fallback
Confidence is calibrated on a versioned domain dataset. Below a threshold, the route service requests clarification,
applies a deterministic safe default, or sends the request to human triage. High confidence does not bypass policy. For
privileged routes, both route eligibility and exact invocation authorization are required.

Anti-patterns
     Persona routing: selecting among nearly identical prompts with no different capability boundary.
     Handoff by chat message: telling another agent “you own this now” without durable state.
     Privilege through selection: showing a model an admin route and treating its selection as authorization.
     No fallback: forcing a best guess even when the request is out of distribution.

Grounding, corrections, and evidence boundary
    EVIDENCE BOUNDARY This chapter presents an architectural recommendation, not a protocol mandate. Validate
    the pattern against the workload, threat model, evaluation set, and strongest simpler baseline.


Evidence anchors: OAI-TOOLS; A2A-101. Full URLs, authority, version, and scope appear in Appendix M.




                                                               BOOK v1.0.0       |   PAGE 55
                                                                    ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Pattern reference — Routing
    PROBLEM Different requests need different workflows, models, Skills, agents, risk controls, or human queues.



Architecture and flow
A classifier proposes a route from a precomputed eligible catalog. A deterministic validator checks confidence, policy,
health, capacity, and fallback before selection.
111.      Build the eligible route set.
112.      Classify the request and return route, confidence, and reason.
113.      Validate thresholds and constraints.
114.      Select the route or deterministic fallback.
115.      Measure outcome by route and slice.

12 — Planning and Plan–Execute
     Evidence note. Published engineering guidance distinguishes predefined workflows from model-directed agents
     and recommends adding complexity only when simpler compositions are insufficient. [R27]

  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT A supplier with four subsidiaries, missing tax evidence, and two regional checks
  requires adaptive sequencing. Northstar lets a planner propose a graph but compiles that graph before any node
  becomes schedulable.



  DESIGN DECISION Use a typed Plan IR and deterministic compiler; freeze committed past work across plan
  revisions.




What this chapter delivers
Compile model-proposed plans into authorized, resource-feasible, replayable execution graphs.

Solution blueprint
Figure 6. Planning and Plan–Execute.
State owner                Trust boundary           Scale unit                    Failure containment          Core contracts

Plan Service and durable   planner proposal to                                    one node and one plan        CandidatePlan ·
                                                    plan node · workload queue
workflow                   deterministic compiler                                 revision                     CompiledPlan · PlanRevision




Planning is compilation, not permission
The planner describes desired work; the compiler determines whether that work is legal and executable. Northstar Plan
IR nodes name capability requirements rather than concrete credentials. Compilation binds each requirement to an
eligible Skill and tool release, verifies input/output schemas, calculates critical path and maximum cost, checks graph
and terminal properties, and emits a signed immutable plan revision.
Resource feasibility matters. A valid graph can still miss the deadline if it requires too many browser sessions, serialized
vendor calls, or human approvals. The compiler checks workload queues, concurrency classes, rate limits, regional
placement, and time windows before committing a plan. Scheduling remains a workflow responsibility.

Replanning contract
New evidence can invalidate future work. Replanning starts from an explicit set of immutable facts: accepted artifacts,
committed effects, approval decisions, and current resource versions. The new revision includes a diff, rationale,
superseded nodes, reused artifacts, changed budget, and compiler evidence. It cannot rewrite history.

Planning, dynamic execution, and deterministic boundaries

                                                                   Allow useful adaptation without letting the model rewrite policy, invent
Problem                                                            capabilities, or turn every step into expensive nondeterministic
                                                                   reasoning.

                                                                   A plan is based on incomplete information; tool results change the
Why it is difficult                                                world; models confuse a coherent narrative with a feasible,
                                                                   authorized execution graph.

                                                                   Fixed workflow; rules engine; model router; plan-and-execute; ReAct
Design alternatives
                                                                   loop; graph search; planner/executor/critic; code generation.

A deterministic compiler rejects cycles unless explicitly permitted, missing schemas, unbounded loops, unauthorized
capabilities, budget overflow, inconsistent data classification, and unhandled terminal paths. A dynamic node may
choose among an allowlisted capability set; it cannot call arbitrary network endpoints.

Compile-time checks
     Every node has a pinned implementation or an allowlisted runtime selector, typed inputs/outputs, timeout, retry
      owner, budget, risk class, acceptance rule, and terminal failure path.
     Data-flow classification can stay equal or become more restrictive. A node cannot send restricted data to a capability
      whose manifest/region/purpose is incompatible.
     Mutating nodes require idempotency/effect semantics and any policy-required preview/approval/compensation. R4
      commit remains a deterministic domain operation.
     Loops require a deterministic guard plus maximum iterations, wall time, cost, and no-progress detector. Delegation
      depth and parallel fan-out are statically bounded.
     The compiler estimates critical path, maximum fan-out, worst-case calls/cost, and required quota. Admission may
      reject, defer, or require approval before commit.

Runtime revision algorithm
126.       Persist the triggering observation/failure and current state version.
127.       Freeze completed effects and accepted artifacts; mark nodes whose assumptions changed as invalidated.
128.       Ask the planner only for the affected horizon, not the entire run.
129.       Compile and diff the candidate against the committed revision.
130.       Reuse nodes only when implementation, inputs, policy, data revisions, and acceptance remain compatible.
131.       Obtain new consent/approval if risk, purpose, destination, spend, or authority expands.
132.       Atomically commit the revision and reservations, then schedule newly ready nodes.

Scalability and performance
     Planner workers are stateless and queue driven. Plan storage is partitioned by tenant + run; immutable revisions can
      be cached by graph hash.
     Cache tool/agent schemas and compatibility matrices by digest. Policy and health are time-sensitive and must be
      rechecked at commit/dispatch.
     Validate incrementally: a plan revision carries changed nodes/edges, allowing graph, type, cost, and policy analysis
      to recompute only impacted regions.
     The scheduler scans no global table. Workflow events enqueue newly ready node IDs; conditional updates ensure
      each logical node is dispatched once per accepted attempt.
     For large DAGs, store canonical graph blobs by hash and keep indexed node/dependency projections in the
      relational store. Operator views are materialized asynchronously.
     Apply per-run ready-node limits and weighted fair scheduling across tenants. Planning cannot reserve unbounded
      future capacity; reservations expire and are reclaimed.
     Use deterministic routing for common/high-confidence cases and invoke the planner only at entry, material
      uncertainty, or failure boundaries. This reduces model cost and plan churn.

Grounding, corrections, and evidence boundary
    EVIDENCE BOUNDARY This chapter presents an architectural recommendation, not a protocol mandate. Validate
    the pattern against the workload, threat model, evaluation set, and strongest simpler baseline.

Pattern reference — Planning
    PROBLEM The task requires adaptive decomposition or dependency ordering that cannot be fully encoded upfront.



Architecture and flow
A planner emits a typed Plan IR. A deterministic compiler validates graph integrity, capabilities, schemas, risk, effects,
budgets, resources, and terminal paths before the workflow schedules nodes.
133.         Create a CandidatePlan with bounded nodes and dependencies.
134.         Compile against the run envelope and release catalog.
135.         Freeze a plan digest and revision.
136.         Schedule ready nodes through durable workflow state.
137.         Replan only the uncommitted future when observations invalidate assumptions.

decomposition provides measurable value.                            be encoded as deterministic workflows.

Implementation reference
Plan IR compiler
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
    PYTHON

1            from __future__ import annotations
2
3            import hashlib
4            import json
5            import math
6            import re



                                                      BOOK v1.0.0    |   PAGE 63
                                                          ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



7    from dataclasses import dataclass
8
9    from .contracts import CandidatePlan
10
11
12   _SHA256 = re.compile(r"^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$")
13
14
15   @dataclass(frozen=True)
16   class PlanEnvelope:
17       """Deterministic limits used to compile one candidate plan."""
18
19       allowed_capabilities: frozenset[str]
20       maximum_cost_usd: float
21       policy_digest: str
22       maximum_nodes: int = 100
23       maximum_risk: int = 4
24
25       def __post_init__(self) -> None:
26           if not math.isfinite(self.maximum_cost_usd) or self.maximum_cost_usd < 0:
27               raise ValueError("maximum_cost_usd must be finite and non-negative")
28           if self.maximum_nodes < 1:
29               raise ValueError("maximum_nodes must be positive")
30           if not 0 <= self.maximum_risk <= 4:
31               raise ValueError("maximum_risk must be between 0 and 4")
32           if _SHA256.fullmatch(self.policy_digest) is None:
33               raise ValueError("policy_digest must be a canonical sha256 digest")
34
35
36   def _sha256(payload: object) -> str:
37       canonical = json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")).encode()
38       return "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(canonical).hexdigest()
39
40
41   def compile_plan(plan: CandidatePlan, envelope: PlanEnvelope) -> dict[str, object]:
42       """Validate and bind a proposed plan to the policy envelope.
43
44       Node maximum_cost_usd values are conservative reservations. Their sum is
45       the compiled reservation even if runtime branches may be mutually exclusive.
46       Actual spend is tracked separately and can never consume beyond the run grant.
47       """
48
49       if len(plan.nodes) > envelope.maximum_nodes:
50           raise ValueError("node limit exceeded")
51       by_id = {node.id: node for node in plan.nodes}
52       if len(by_id) != len(plan.nodes):
53           raise ValueError("duplicate node ID")
54
55       for node in plan.nodes:
56           if node.capability not in envelope.allowed_capabilities:
57               raise PermissionError(f"capability not allowed: {node.capability}")
58           if node.risk_class > envelope.maximum_risk:
59               raise PermissionError(f"risk ceiling exceeded: {node.id}")
60           unknown = set(node.depends_on) - by_id.keys()
61           if unknown:
62               raise ValueError(f"unknown dependencies for {node.id}: {sorted(unknown)}")
63
64       reserved_cost = sum(node.maximum_cost_usd for node in plan.nodes)
65       if not math.isfinite(reserved_cost) or reserved_cost > envelope.maximum_cost_usd:
66           raise ValueError("plan exceeds maximum cost")
67
68       dependencies = {node.id: set(node.depends_on) for node in plan.nodes}
69       ready = sorted(node_id for node_id, deps in dependencies.items() if not deps)
70       order: list[str] = []
71       while ready:
72           current = ready.pop(0)
73           order.append(current)
74           for node_id, deps in dependencies.items():
75               if current in deps:
76                    deps.remove(current)
77                    if not deps and node_id not in order and node_id not in ready:
78                        ready.append(node_id)
79           ready.sort()



                                                BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 64
                                                                 ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



80            if len(order) != len(plan.nodes):
81                raise ValueError("cycle detected")
82
83            plan_payload = plan.model_dump(mode="json")
84            envelope_payload = {
85                "allowed_capabilities": sorted(envelope.allowed_capabilities),
86                "maximum_cost_usd": envelope.maximum_cost_usd,
87                "maximum_nodes": envelope.maximum_nodes,
88                "maximum_risk": envelope.maximum_risk,
89                "policy_digest": envelope.policy_digest,
90            }
91            return {
92                "revision": plan.revision,
93                "topological_order": tuple(order),
94                "reserved_cost_usd": reserved_cost,
95                "cost_semantics": "sum_of_per_node_maximum_reservations",
96                "policy_digest": envelope.policy_digest,
97                "plan_digest": _sha256(plan_payload),
98                "compiled_plan_digest": _sha256(
99                     {"plan": plan_payload, "envelope": envelope_payload}
100               ),
101           }




Debugging playbook
138.       Re-run the plan compiler against the exact release and policy versions.
139.       Find the first node whose capability, budget, or schema became invalid.
140.       Compare plan revisions and ensure committed facts did not change.

Production checklist
✓ Model planning is used only for genuine semantic uncertainty.
✓ Plans are typed, immutable, versioned graphs with explicit limits and acceptance.
✓ Graph, authority, security, resource, schema, and effect constraints compile before commit.
✓ Completed effects and accepted artifacts are frozen across revisions.
✓ Revisions, loops, fan-out, depth, time, cost, and no-progress behavior are bounded.

Technical review questions
Question                                                             Strong answer

                                                                     When paths, invariants, and recovery semantics are known; use
When should you use a deterministic workflow instead of model
                                                                     planning only for real semantic uncertainty or environmental
planning?
                                                                     adaptation.

                                                                     It makes nodes, dependencies, capabilities, schemas, risk, budgets,
Why create a typed Plan IR?                                          and failure behavior inspectable, compilable, versionable, and
                                                                     schedulable.

                                                                     Graph integrity, schema compatibility, authority subset, data and
What must a plan compiler validate?                                  region policy, effect and approval semantics, capacity, cost,
                                                                     deadlines, and terminal paths.

                                                                     Treat committed effects and accepted artifacts as immutable facts;
How should replanning handle completed work?                         revise only the affected future horizon and append a new version with
                                                                     rationale and diff.

                                                                     External revision, loop, step, cost, time, delegation, and no-progress
How do you prevent plausible but endless planning?
                                                                     limits plus deterministic acceptance and terminal transitions.

13 — Reflection and Evaluator–Optimizer
  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT The first recommendation is coherent but omits a contradictory address and
  overstates confidence. Northstar adds bounded reflection for revision and an independently defined evaluator for
  acceptance.



  DESIGN DECISION Use reflection for targeted revision and independent, calibrated acceptance for protected
  outcomes.




What this chapter delivers
Use critique and evaluation to improve outputs without self-certification, unbounded loops, or hidden cost.

Solution blueprint
Figure 7. Reflection and Evaluator–Optimizer.
State owner             Trust boundary            Scale unit                        Failure containment   Core contracts

                        generator/evaluator and                                                           EvaluationResult · Rubric ·
Acceptance Engine                                 candidate · scorer pool · slice   one revision cycle
                        evaluator/commit                                                                  AcceptanceDecision




Improvement without self-certification
Reflection asks, “What is missing or inconsistent?” Evaluation asks, “Does this candidate meet an acceptance rubric?”
Combining them can improve complex artifacts, but only if the roles are explicit and bounded. Northstar runs executable
checks before any model judge: schema, evidence presence, policy, effect state, resource version, arithmetic, and
deterministic domain rules.
Qualitative criteria—clarity, completeness, grounded synthesis, or policy-explanation quality—may use calibrated judges.
Judge prompt, model route, examples, sampling parameters, and rubric are versioned. Northstar measures variance,
human agreement, and failure slices. A model score is evidence for a release or review decision; it is not permission to
access data or commit a transaction.

Bounded optimization loop
Each revision targets structured feedback and carries a diff. The loop stops on pass, maximum revisions, maximum
spend, deadline, or no-progress. If the evaluator and generator repeatedly disagree, the workflow escalates with both
artifacts rather than silently choosing the more persuasive prose.

Grounding, corrections, and evidence boundary
  EVIDENCE BOUNDARY This chapter presents an architectural recommendation, not a protocol mandate. Validate
  the pattern against the workload, threat model, evaluation set, and strongest simpler baseline.


Evidence anchors: MLFLOW-EVAL; NIST-GAI. Full URLs, authority, version, and scope appear in Appendix M.

Pattern reference — Reflection
  PROBLEM A first candidate may contain omissions, contradictions, or weak reasoning that targeted critique can
  expose.




                                                  BOOK v1.0.0       |   PAGE 67
                                                                      ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Architecture and flow
A reflector reviews a candidate against explicit evidence and rubric, emits structured defects, and proposes a bounded
revision. It does not commit or certify protected outcomes.
143.      Generate a candidate.
144.      Compare it with evidence and rubric.
145.      Emit defect types and references.
146.      Revise only supported defects.
147.      Stop on pass, no-progress, or revision budget.
Use when                                                                Do not use when

Use when measured offline results show critique improves a specific     Avoid if correctness is executable, the first pass already meets it, or
quality dimension.                                                      added latency has no measured benefit.

Pattern reference — Evaluator–Optimizer
    PROBLEM A generated artifact must reach a quality threshold that mixes executable and semantic criteria.



Architecture and flow
A generator creates candidates; deterministic scorers and calibrated evaluators produce typed feedback; an optimizer
revises within a strict iteration budget; an acceptance engine decides.
148.      Generate candidate v1.
149.      Run deterministic checks first.
150.      Run calibrated qualitative evaluation if needed.
151.      Apply actionable feedback to one revision.
152.      Accept, reject, or escalate at a bounded threshold.
Use when                                                                Do not use when

Use for documents, plans, code, or analyses where iterative quality     Do not use a model judge as sole authority for permissions,
gain is measurable and latency permits.                                 transaction success, security, or computable correctness.

Implementation reference
Bounded evaluator–optimizer
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
    PYTHON

1            from __future__ import annotations
2            import hashlib
3            import json
4            from dataclasses import dataclass
5            from typing import Awaitable, Callable, Protocol
6            from .contracts import AgentDecision
7
8            class Evaluation:
9                accepted: bool
10               score: float
11               feedback: str
12
13           class EvaluatorOptimizer:
14               def __init__(
15                   self, generator: Generator, evaluator: Evaluator, maximum_revisions: int = 2
16               ) -> None:
17                   self.generator = generator
18                   self.evaluator = evaluator
19                   self.maximum_revisions = maximum_revisions
20
21                async def run(self, task: str) -> tuple[str, Evaluation]:
22                    feedback: str | None = None
23                    for _ in range(self.maximum_revisions + 1):
24                        candidate = await self.generator(task, feedback)
25                        evaluation = await self.evaluator(task, candidate)
26                        if evaluation.accepted:
27                            return candidate, evaluation
28                        feedback = evaluation.feedback
29                    raise RuntimeError("candidate did not pass bounded evaluation")

Production checklist
✓ Generator and evaluator roles, prompts, models, and evidence are distinguishable.
✓ Computable invariants use code, not a model judge.
✓ Revision count, spend, time, and no-progress are bounded.
✓ Judge calibration and inter-rater agreement are measured.
✓ Failed candidates and feedback enter a regression corpus.

14 — Human-in-the-Loop
     Evidence note. Human-review interfaces should be tested against WCAG 2.2 accessibility criteria; accessibility
     conformance does not by itself eliminate automation bias or establish decision fairness. [R41]

  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT A protected supplier activation requires approval. An approver must see the exact
  action, destination, evidence, expected resource version, and risk—not a vague request to “approve the agent.”



  DESIGN DECISION Persist proposal-bound approvals as durable workflow events and reauthorize immediately
  before commit.




What this chapter delivers
Bind approval and intervention to immutable proposals and durable state transitions.

Solution blueprint
Figure 8. Human-in-the-Loop.
State owner                Trust boundary              Scale unit                      Failure containment          Core contracts

                                                                                                                    ApprovalRequest ·
Workflow plus Approval     machine proposal to human   approval queue · risk class ·
                                                                                       one immutable proposal       ApprovalDecision ·
Service                    authority                   tenant
                                                                                                                    EffectReceipt




Two distinct human controls
Approval authorizes one proposed effect. Intervention asks a person to diagnose, correct, supply information, or
choose among unresolved alternatives. Combining them creates audit ambiguity. Northstar uses separate queues, roles,
forms, SLOs, and terminal semantics.
The Approval Service stores a canonical proposal, human-readable preview, destination, evidence and policy
references, risk, expected resource version, expiry, and proposal hash. Approval becomes invalid when any protected
field changes. A commit path rechecks approver authority, separation of duties, current policy, grant freshness, and
resource version.
Human capacity is finite. Queue age, dwell, abandonment, reassignment, disagreement, and after-hours coverage are
production metrics. A system that requires routine approval for low-risk deterministic work may be less safe because
reviewers become fatigued.

Human-in-the-loop and operational control
Required analysis                                                       Design conclusion

                                                                        Humans must understand, approve, correct, or stop consequential
Problem
                                                                        work without becoming a rubber stamp or breaking run integrity.

                                                                        Too many approvals cause fatigue; too little context prevents
Why it is difficult                                                     informed decisions; approvals can go stale as data or plans change;
                                                                        edits must be attributable and replayable.

                                                                        Confirm every tool call; risk-based approvals; maker-checker;
Design alternatives
                                                                        sampled review; exception-only review; fully manual commit.

                                                                        Use policy-triggered gates based on risk, novelty, confidence, value,
                                                                        data sensitivity, reversibility, and destination. Present a concise
Recommended architecture                                                preview, evidence, alternatives, changed fields, policy rationale, and
                                                                        blast radius. Approval authorizes a specific immutable proposal for a
                                                                        limited time.



                                                       BOOK v1.0.0       |   PAGE 71
                                                               ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Required analysis                                                  Design conclusion

                                                                   Human review adds latency and staffing cost but provides judgment
Trade-offs                                                         and accountability where automation evidence is insufficient. Poor
                                                                   UX converts the control into theater.

                                                                   Approval applies to a changed plan; approver lacks authority; agent
Failure scenarios                                                  hides uncertainty; request times out; two operators issue conflicting
                                                                   overrides; “approve all” becomes normal.

                                                                   A customer-service agent drafts a high-value refund. The supervisor
Enterprise example                                                 sees policy basis, customer history, amount, downstream effect, and
                                                                   alternatives; any amount or recipient change invalidates the approval.



Approval contract
An ApprovalRequest contains: proposal/artifact hash, exact action and resource, before/after diff, subject and actor,
evidence links, uncertainty, policy rule, risk class, estimated impact/cost, reversible flag, expiry, separation-of-duties
requirement, and allowed responses (approve, reject, edit-and-resubmit, request-evidence, cancel-run).

Operator console requirements
     Live run graph with current owner, state, budgets, queues, waits, child tasks, and last committed effect.
     Redacted but causally complete trace; model/tool inputs available only to authorized incident roles.
     Pause, cancel, retry safe activity, skip with justification, patch plan, reassign approval, increase budget, trigger
      compensation, and fork a sandbox replay.
     Conflict detection and optimistic versions for operator edits.
     Immutable operator identity, reason, timestamp, before/after state, and linked ticket on every override.
     SLA timers and escalation paths; a timed-out approval follows explicit policy, never implicit approval.

A decision is valid only when the presented proposal hash equals the current proposal, the approver is authorized at
decision time, required counts/separation rules are satisfied, and the approval has not expired or been superseded.

Workflow integration
158.    The workflow commits the proposed effect and evidence snapshot, then creates the approval.
159.    The Approval Service registers durable SLA/expiry timers and returns a wait correlation ID.
160.    Assignment and notifications may retry independently; the workflow remains suspended without a worker.
161.    A decision transaction records the immutable decision and emits an outbox event.
162.    The workflow receives the event, revalidates policy/current resource version, and commits only the proposal
    hash that was approved.
163.    If the resource, amount, recipient, scope, or policy changed materially, the workflow supersedes the approval
    and generates a fresh preview.

Scalability and operations
     Partition approvals by tenant + business queue/region while keeping a direct index by approval/run ID. Strongly
      serialize decisions for one approval; scale independently across approvals.
     Use workflow timers or a durable timer service for SLA/escalation—never scan an approvals table each minute at
      enterprise scale.
     Materialize queue views by eligibility dimensions and age. Compute final authorization at read/claim/decision time so
      an index cannot leak work after role changes.
     Coalesce notifications and support digest/batched review for low-risk homogeneous actions, but preserve one
      proposal and decision record per effect.



                                                    BOOK v1.0.0    |   PAGE 73
                                                                        ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



     Apply queue admission and staffing metrics: arrival rate, service time, oldest age, abandonment, reassignment,
      rejection, and correction. Approval capacity is part of end-to-end capacity planning.
     Prevent approval stampedes during incidents with per-queue rate limits, prioritized critical items, bulk-action policy,
      and explicit incident-mode controls—never an unbounded “approve all.”
     Replicate redacted queue projections for availability, but keep the authoritative decision write in the run’s home/legal
      region. After failover, fence the prior writer before accepting decisions.
     Cache immutable evidence by hash and render role-specific views on demand; large source material remains in
      governed artifact storage.

                                                  policy, authority, proposal hash, expiry,
Approval integrity                                destination, and resource version               OAI-HITL; OWASP-AGENT
                                                  immediately before commit. Never transport
                                                  serialized run state inside exception text or
                                                  ordinary logs.

Architecture and flow
The workflow persists a proposal-bound ApprovalRequest or InterventionTask, pauses, receives an authenticated

Implementation reference
Proposal-bound approval contract
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
    PYTHON

1            from __future__ import annotations
2
3            import hashlib
4            import json
5            from datetime import datetime
6            from pydantic import BaseModel
7
8
9            class ApprovalRequest(BaseModel):
10               approval_id: str
11               run_id: str
12               action: str
13               destination: str
14               canonical_arguments: dict[str, object]
15               evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...]
16               expected_resource_version: str
17               expires_at: datetime
18               proposal_sha256: str
19
20
21           def proposal_hash(action: str, destination: str,
22                             arguments: dict[str, object],
23                             evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...],
24                             expected_version: str) -> str:
25               body = {
26                   "action": action,
27                   "destination": destination,
28                   "arguments": arguments,
29                   "evidence_ids": evidence_ids,
30                   "expected_version": expected_version,
31               }
32               encoded = json.dumps(body, sort_keys=True,
33                                    separators=(",", ":")).encode()
34               return "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(encoded).hexdigest()



Executable proposal-bound approval
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
    PYTHON

1            from __future__ import annotations
2
3            import hashlib
4            import json
5            from datetime import datetime
6            from typing import Any
7
8            from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator, model_validator
9
10




                                                     BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 75
                                                       ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



11   def _require_aware(value: datetime, field_name: str) -> datetime:
12       if value.tzinfo is None or value.utcoffset() is None:
13           raise ValueError(f"{field_name} must be timezone-aware")
14       return value
15
16
17   class ActionProposal(BaseModel):
18       """Exact action reviewed by an approver; immutable after presentation."""
19
20       tenant_id: str
21       run_id: str
22       actor_id: str
23       tool_version: str
24       logical_effect_key: str
25       arguments: dict[str, Any]
26       expected_resource_version: int
27       policy_digest: str
28       expires_at: datetime
29
30       @field_validator("expires_at")
31       @classmethod
32       def expiry_is_aware(cls, value: datetime) -> datetime:
33           return _require_aware(value, "expires_at")
34
35       def digest(self) -> str:
36           payload = json.dumps(
37               self.model_dump(mode="json"), sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")
38           ).encode()
39           return "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(payload).hexdigest()
40
41
42   class ApprovalGrant(BaseModel):
43       approval_id: str
44       proposal_hash: str
45       tenant_id: str
46       run_id: str
47       approver_id: str
48       approved_at: datetime
49       expires_at: datetime
50       policy_digest: str
51       obligations: tuple[str, ...] = Field(default_factory=tuple)
52
53       @field_validator("approved_at", "expires_at")
54       @classmethod
55       def timestamps_are_aware(cls, value: datetime, info) -> datetime:
56           return _require_aware(value, info.field_name)
57
58       @model_validator(mode="after")
59       def expiry_follows_approval(self) -> "ApprovalGrant":
60           if self.expires_at <= self.approved_at:
61               raise ValueError("expires_at must be later than approved_at")
62           return self
63
64
65   def verify_approval(
66       proposal: ActionProposal,
67       grant: ApprovalGrant,
68       *,
69       now: datetime,
70       actual_resource_version: int,
71   ) -> None:
72       """Fail closed if approval no longer matches the action or current state."""
73
74       _require_aware(now, "now")
75       if grant.proposal_hash != proposal.digest():
76           raise PermissionError("approval is not bound to this proposal")
77       if (grant.tenant_id, grant.run_id) != (proposal.tenant_id, proposal.run_id):
78           raise PermissionError("approval scope mismatch")
79       if grant.approver_id == proposal.actor_id:
80           raise PermissionError("actor cannot self-approve")
81       if grant.policy_digest != proposal.policy_digest:
82           raise PermissionError("policy changed after approval")
83       if now < grant.approved_at:



                                             BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 76
                                                                ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



84                raise PermissionError("approval is not yet effective")
85            if now >= min(grant.expires_at, proposal.expires_at):
86                raise PermissionError("approval expired")
87            if actual_resource_version != proposal.expected_resource_version:
88                raise PermissionError("resource changed after approval")

Production checklist
✓ Every capability has an immutable manifest covering interface, security, risk, effects, runtime, ownership, and lifecycle.
✓ Discovery is minimal and exact resource authorization happens at invocation.
✓ Mutations have logical keys, intent hashes, preconditions, receipts, reconciliation, compensation, and one retry owner.
✓ Approval binds to an immutable proposal hash and current authority and resource state.
✓ Reconciliation, compensation, pause, cancel, and intervention are governed commands.

Technical review questions
Question                                                           Strong answer

                                                                   Central schema, policy, credentials, rate limits, risk, approval, effect
Why put a Tool Gateway between agents and enterprise systems?
                                                                   semantics, result sanitation, observability, and lifecycle enforcement.

                                                                   Eligibility is temporary and often coarse; the gateway must check
Why is tool discovery not authorization?                           current subject, actor, purpose, resource, arguments, risk, and policy
                                                                   immediately before execution.

                                                                   Persist the exact canonical proposal, preview, destination, evidence,
How should approval be bound to an action?                         risk, and hash; authorize the approver; reject expiry, supersession,
                                                                   hash mismatch, or stale resource state.

                                                                   Idempotency prevents duplicate logical intent; expected versions
Why are both idempotency and optimistic concurrency needed?
                                                                   prevent an otherwise valid action from overwriting newer state.

15 — Long-Running and Durable Agents
    Evidence note. Durable-execution systems preserve workflow progress across failures and long waits; concrete
    integrations and replay semantics vary by backend. [R02][R36][R38]

  NORTHSTAR AT THIS POINT The supplier waits nine days to upload a certificate. During the wait, workers deploy
  twice, policy changes, a user loses a role, and one earlier external request timed out. The run must resume safely
  rather than restart from a prompt transcript.



  DESIGN DECISION Represent long-running work as replay-safe workflow history plus bounded activities, durable
  waits, and reconciliation.




What this chapter delivers
Preserve weeks-long work across waits, failures, deployments, retries, cancellations, and uncertain effects.

Solution blueprint
Figure 9. Long-Running and Durable Agents.
State owner               Trust boundary                  Scale unit                         Failure containment      Core contracts

                          workflow activity to external   history shard · activity queue ·                            WorkflowEvent · Checkpoint ·
Durable workflow engine                                                                      one workflow execution
                          I/O                             timer                                                       WaitRegistration




Durable state is more than chat history
Chat history cannot reliably encode timers, retries, child ownership, approval waits, cancellation intent, resource
versions, effect receipts, or deployment compatibility. Northstar uses event history to rebuild a canonical RunState;
model and tool calls are bounded activities whose inputs and outputs are recorded by reference.
The workflow reducer must be deterministic. It cannot read the clock, call a model, query a database, generate
randomness, or perform network I/O during replay. Those operations become activities, signals, or recorded events. This
separation makes a crashed worker replaceable.

Terminal correctness
A run reaches Succeeded only through executable acceptance. A canceled run with a possibly committed write does not
simply disappear; it moves through reconciliation and possibly compensation. If correctness cannot be established
automatically, InterventionRequired is a valid terminal operational state.

Execution lifecycle
Canonical run state machine
Canonical control flow
174.    Admit. Authenticate the caller; bind tenant, subject, channel, purpose, data classification, requested autonomy,
    and a client idempotency key.
175.    Snapshot. Resolve immutable versions of the agent, workflow, prompt/skill bundle, policy, model route, tool
    schemas, and knowledge index.
176.    Authorize. Evaluate the requested goal and maximum capability envelope; allocate token, cost, time, tool,
    delegation, and concurrency budgets.
177.    Plan. The model may propose a typed plan. Code validates DAG constraints, allowed capabilities, risk classes,
    preconditions, and budgets.



                                                          BOOK v1.0.0        |   PAGE 78
                                                           ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



178.    Execute. The workflow kernel schedules replay-safe activities. Each effect is individually authorized using fresh
    context and a downscoped credential.
179.    Checkpoint. Persist state after every externally visible effect and meaningful decision; store large artifacts by
    immutable reference and hash.
180.    Wait or intervene. Suspend without a worker for timers, callbacks, clarification, or approval. Signals carry
    correlation and authorization metadata.
181.    Validate and commit. Deterministic validators test outputs and invariants. High-risk effects use propose →
    preview → approve → commit.
182.    Complete. Produce a typed result, evidence bundle, final cost/quality metrics, retention class, and terminal
    event.
183.    Learn safely. Feed de-identified traces and failures into curated datasets; never let production memory or
    prompts self-modify without review.

Long-running agents and event-driven execution
Required analysis                                              Design conclusion

                                                               Runs lasting minutes to months must survive crashes, deployments,
Problem                                                        expired credentials, human waits, rate limits, and external callbacks
                                                               without restarting from scratch.

                                                               Process memory is ephemeral; replay can repeat nondeterministic
                                                               calls and side effects; external systems deliver duplicates or
Why it is difficult
                                                               callbacks out of order; code and schemas evolve while runs are
                                                               active.

                                                               Background job + database; queue consumers; graph checkpointing;
                                                               cloud state machines; durable execution engines such as

                                                               acknowledgment; old run cannot replay after code change; callback

                                                               approvals. The workflow is unloaded while idle, resumes on
Enterprise example

                                                               last committed effect.



Durable execution contract
    Workflow code: deterministic control logic only. No wall-clock reads, random values, direct network calls, or model
     calls unless captured as replay-safe activities.
    Activity: one bounded external interaction with timeout, retry class, idempotency key, heartbeat, cancellation
     behavior, and structured result.
    Checkpoint: workflow-state snapshot plus event-history position, plan version, effect ledger cursor, artifact
     references, and version pins.
    Signal: authenticated external input (approval.granted, document.received, callback.completed, user.clarified)
     carrying a correlation key and monotonic event ID.
    Query: read-only inspection of run state for users and operators; must not advance execution.
    Update: authorized mutation such as budget increase, plan patch, assignee change, or safe retry.




                                                 BOOK v1.0.0    |   PAGE 79
                                                            ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Checkpoint policy
Checkpoint after every irreversible or externally visible effect, before long waits, after plan revisions, and at fan-in
boundaries. Do not serialize full prompt histories into every checkpoint; persist canonical state and immutable artifact
references. Encrypt by tenant and apply retention by data class.

Event-driven resume flow
Event architecture
Use a CloudEvents-compatible envelope with id, source, type, subject, time, dataschema, tenant\_id, correlation\_id,
causation\_id, traceparent, and a payload reference. Keep sensitive or large content in an access-controlled object store;
events carry references and hashes. Use transactional outbox on producers and inbox/deduplication on consumers. A
dead-letter queue is quarantine and diagnosis—not a substitute for domain compensation.

Detailed design — APIs, data model, and scalability
Component boundaries
The Run API persists intent and returns quickly; the durable workflow owns lifecycle; activity workers perform model/tool
I/O; Event Ingress authenticates external facts; the Correlator maps those facts to waiting runs; Timer/Signal services
wake workflows; and a Version Router keeps compatible workflow builds available for in-flight histories.

High-level APIs
API                                                             Purpose and contract

                                                                Idempotent admission; returns 202, run ID, home region, pinned

                                                                Read a materialized projection with state\_version, waits, deadlines,
GET /v1/runs/{run\_id}
                                                                budget, and latest checkpoint

                                                                Deliver typed user/system signal with event ID, correlation key,
POST /v1/runs/{run\_id}:signal
                                                                schema version, subject authorization, and expected state

                                                                Authenticate a CloudEvent, store inbox receipt, then route to a
POST /v1/events:ingest
                                                                waiting workflow or unmatched-event holding area

Execution and persistence rules
     Record model and external tool calls as activities whose results are persisted before workflow continuation. Replay
      returns the recorded result; it does not recall the model or tool.
     Persist a wait registration before acknowledging that the run is waiting. Event Ingress writes the inbox receipt before
      signaling the workflow, closing the “event arrived before subscription” race.
     For a side effect, persist intent/effect key before invocation and reconcile the receipt before an activity is considered
      complete.
     Use logical workflow time for decisions. Wall-clock access, random IDs, and dynamic configuration are provided as
      recorded events/side effects.
     Continue-as-new or history compaction starts a new generation with a verified state snapshot when history size or
      event count reaches an engine-specific threshold; preserve an audit link to prior generations.

Scalability and multi-region behavior
     Shard workflows by stable run ID and timers by time bucket + shard. Activity queues are separate from workflow-
      task queues so slow tools cannot starve state transitions.
     Waiting executions consume storage, not workers. Capacity planning distinguishes active workflow tasks, scheduled
      activities, open waits, timer cardinality, and history-write throughput.
     Autoscale activity workers on oldest-message age, arrival rate, observed service time, provider quota, and
      downstream health. CPU is only a secondary signal for I/O-heavy agent tasks.
     Use sticky execution caches for latency but require correctness without them; any compatible worker must rebuild
      state from history after eviction or failure.
     Assign a run to one home region. Replicate encrypted history and projections for DR, but allow only one writer
      region per generation. On failover, fence the old region, restore/verify history, reconcile external effects, then
      resume.
     Separate task queues by workflow build compatibility. Keep old workers available for in-flight runs or migrate
      explicitly; never replay old history against untested new code.
     Rate-limit external signals per tenant/source and coalesce superseding updates where domain semantics allow.
      Preserve individual inbox receipts even when only the latest value wakes the workflow.

Grounding, corrections, and evidence boundary
Claim or correction                        Grounded statement                              Source IDs

                                           Durability is incomplete without measured
                                           RTO/RPO, compatible worker availability,
Recovery objective                         backup restoration, region failover, and        TEMPORAL; CLOUDEVENTS
                                           explicit treatment of pending approvals and
                                           unknown effects during disaster recovery.


Evidence anchors: TEMPORAL; CLOUDEVENTS. Full URLs, authority, version, and scope appear in Appendix M.

Pattern reference — Long-Running Agents
    PROBLEM Business work lasts longer than a process lifetime and must survive waits, worker loss, retries,
    deployments, and partial external failures.




                                                     BOOK v1.0.0    |   PAGE 81
                                                                     ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Architecture and flow
A durable workflow records deterministic decisions and schedules bounded activities. Timers, signals, children,
approvals, effects, and release pins are durable history.
184.         Admit and pin the run manifest.
185.         Record each state transition before scheduling work.
186.         Execute models and tools as bounded idempotent activities.
187.         Persist waits and resume through authenticated signals or timers.
188.         Reconcile effects and verify terminal criteria before closing.
Use when                                                                 Do not use when

Use for work with waits, multiple systems, protected effects,            Avoid a workflow engine for a single short idempotent read with no
independent failures, or duration beyond one request.                    recovery or coordination requirement.



Trade-offs
Choice                                                                   Consequence

Failure recovery                                                         Requires deterministic workflow discipline and history compatibility.

                                                                         Preserves state; creates lifecycle, retention, and migration
Long waits
                                                                         obligations.



Failures and controls
Failure                                                                  Control

Nondeterministic replay                                                  Move I/O, clocks, randomness, and model calls to activities.

Stuck wait                                                               Durable deadline, accepted signal sources, escalation, and visibility.

Unknown mutation                                                         Freeze dependents and reconcile before retry or close.



Minimum observability
      workflow build
      history event
      activity attempt
      timer and wait state
      signal source
      effect reconciliation
      terminal reason

Implementation reference
Replay-safe workflow reducer
PYTHON · implementation excerpt
    PYTHON

1            from __future__ import annotations
2
3            from dataclasses import dataclass, replace
4            from typing import Literal
5
6            RunStatus = Literal["submitted", "running", "waiting", "needs_approval",
7                                "succeeded", "failed", "canceled", "intervention_required"]
8            TERMINAL = frozenset({"succeeded", "failed", "canceled", "intervention_required"})
9
10           @dataclass(frozen=True)
11           class RunState:
12               status: RunStatus = "submitted"
13               state_version: int = 0
14               current_node: str | None = None



                                                           BOOK v1.0.0    |   PAGE 82
                                                       ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



15       cancel_requested: bool = False
16       unknown_effect_ids: tuple[str, ...] = ()
17       terminal_reason: str | None = None
18
19   @dataclass(frozen=True)
20   class WorkflowEvent:
21       kind: str
22       data: dict[str, object]
23       expected_state_version: int | None = None
24
25
26   def evolve(state: RunState, event: WorkflowEvent) -> RunState:
27       if event.expected_state_version is not None and event.expected_state_version != state.state_version:
28           raise ValueError("stale workflow event")
29       if state.status in TERMINAL:
30           raise ValueError("terminal workflow cannot transition")
31       version = state.state_version + 1
32       if event.kind == "RunAuthorized" and state.status == "submitted":
33           start = str(event.data["start_node"])
34           return replace(state, status="running", current_node=start, state_version=version)
35       if event.kind == "ActivityCompleted" and state.status == "running":
36           return replace(state, current_node=str(event.data["next_node"]), state_version=version)
37       if event.kind == "WaitStarted" and state.status == "running":
38           return replace(state, status="waiting", state_version=version)
39       if event.kind == "WaitSatisfied" and state.status == "waiting":
40           return replace(state, status="running", state_version=version)
41       if event.kind == "ApprovalRequired" and state.status == "running":
42           return replace(state, status="needs_approval", state_version=version)
43       if event.kind == "ApprovalReceived" and state.status == "needs_approval":
44           return replace(state, status="running", state_version=version)
45       if event.kind == "EffectUnknown":
46           effect_id = str(event.data["effect_id"])
47           if effect_id in state.unknown_effect_ids:
48               return replace(state, state_version=version)
49           return replace(state, unknown_effect_ids=state.unknown_effect_ids + (effect_id,),
     state_version=version)
50       if event.kind == "EffectReconciled":
51           effect_id = str(event.data["effect_id"])
52           return replace(state, unknown_effect_ids=tuple(e for e in state.unknown_effect_ids if e !=
     effect_id), state_version=version)
53       if event.kind == "CancelRequested":
54           return replace(state, cancel_requested=True, state_version=version)
55       if event.kind == "RunSucceeded" and state.status == "running":
56           if state.unknown_effect_ids:
57               raise ValueError("cannot succeed with unknown effects")
58           return replace(state, status="succeeded", terminal_reason="accepted", state_version=version)
59       if event.kind == "RunFailed":
60           return replace(state, status="failed", terminal_reason=str(event.data["reason"]),
     state_version=version)
61       if event.kind == "InterventionRequired":
62           return replace(state, status="intervention_required", terminal_reason=str(event.data["reason"]),
     state_version=version)
63       raise ValueError(f"invalid event {event.kind!r} from state {state.status!r}")
64
65
66   def decide(state: RunState) -> list[dict[str, object]]:
67       if state.unknown_effect_ids:
68           return [{"type": "reconcile_effect", "effect_id": e} for e in state.unknown_effect_ids]
69       if state.cancel_requested:
70           return [{"type": "close_canceled"}]
71       if state.status in TERMINAL or state.status in {"waiting", "needs_approval", "submitted"}:
72           return []
73       if state.status == "running" and state.current_node:
74           return [{"type": "schedule_activity", "node": state.current_node}]
75       return [{"type": "require_intervention", "reason": "running workflow has no current node"}]
76
77
78   def replay(history: list[WorkflowEvent]) -> RunState:
79       state = RunState()
80       for event in history:
81           state = evolve(state, event)
82       return state




                                             BOOK v1.0.0     |   PAGE 83
                                                                   ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Debugging playbook
189.       Replay workflow history with the pinned build and locate nondeterminism.
190.       Inspect pending timers, signals, activities, and child tasks.
191.       Resolve unknown effects before replaying dependent commands.

Production checklist
✓ Workflow decisions are replay-safe; I/O, clocks, randomness, models, and tools are recorded activities.
✓ Waits, signals, timers, cancellation, and child lifecycles are durable protocol state.
✓ Each failure class has one retry owner; unknown effects route to reconciliation.
✓ Active histories stay pinned to compatible workflow builds and activity contracts.
✓ Workflow tasks, activities, timers, waits, history writes, and reconciliation are capacity-planned separately.

Exercises
192.       Model a ten-day wait with role revocation and policy change.
193.       Design cancellation semantics for a run with a possibly committed external write.

Technical review questions
Question                                                               Strong answer

                                                                       Control decisions and waits are recorded as replayable history;
How is durable execution different from a background job?              workers can disappear without restarting the business process or
                                                                       losing timers and signals.

                                                                       Deterministic coordination in workflow code; models, databases,
What belongs in workflow code versus an activity?                      APIs, tools, clocks, randomness, and other external interactions in
                                                                       bounded activities.

                                                                       Persist a typed wait registration, accepted sources and correlation,
How do you represent a wait that may last weeks?                       deadline, authorization, and resume node; wake through an
                                                                       authenticated signal or durable timer.

                                                                       Persist cancellation intent, stop new work, mark the effect unknown if
What happens if cancellation arrives while a write may have
                                                                       needed, reconcile the source system, then compensate or require
committed?
                                                                       intervention before closing.

                                                                       Preserve compatible workers, use explicit version markers, test
How do you deploy new workflow code safely?                            replay, migrate deterministically when supported, or start a new
                                                                       linked generation when semantics cannot be preserved.

16 — Enterprise Pattern Catalog
How to use the cards
Choose by hidden obligation, uncertainty, and risk—not fashionable terminology. Every card uses one review sequence
so teams can compare alternatives consistently.




                                      Figure 16-1 — Pattern selection decision tree


Reasoning
P-01 — Bounded ReAct
Problem. Adaptive evidence is needed.
Anti-Pattern. Unbounded think–act loop.
Why It Fails. The model owns stopping, budget, and success.
Recommended Pattern. Bound ReAct with step/time/token/tool/evidence limits and deterministic acceptance.
Architecture. Run Service → reason → eligible tool → observation → stop/accept.
Enterprise Example. Supplier screening queries two approved registries, asks once for missing ID, then stops.
Detection Metrics. Steps, no-progress loops, repeat queries, budget exhaustion, evidence coverage.
Trade-offs. Bounds can stop useful exploration; tune by risk.

Orchestration
P-02 — Deterministic spine with semantic nodes
Problem. Known obligations include a few judgment points.
Anti-Pattern. A conversational agent owns the process.
Why It Fails. Retries, waits, joins, approval, and compensation become ambiguous.
Recommended Pattern. Durable workflow with bounded semantic nodes.


                                                 BOOK v1.0.0    |   PAGE 85
                                                              ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



Architecture. Workflow state → semantic task → typed result → deterministic transition.
Enterprise Example. Onboarding always validates, screens, approves, creates, and notifies.
Detection Metrics. Transcript-only state, orphan tasks, duplicate effects, recovery time.
Trade-offs. Workflow modeling adds upfront cost.

Context engineering
P-03 — Typed ContextManifest
Problem. The model needs authorized fresh evidence under budget.
Anti-Pattern. Dump top-k and chat history into the prompt.
Why It Fails. Selection, ACL, freshness, poisoning, and truncation are not reproducible.
Recommended Pattern. Manifest source, ACL, snapshot, provenance, rank, freshness, token allocation.
Architecture. Planner → retrieval → policy → ranking/dedup → manifest → model.
Enterprise Example. Contract review receives clauses from authorized agreements and effective policy.
Detection Metrics. ACL rejects, freshness, recall/precision, lost-position, unsupported claims.
Trade-offs. Snapshotting adds storage and latency.

Memory
P-04 — Provenance-bound tiered memory
Problem. Useful facts must persist safely.
Anti-Pattern. Write every interaction to one vector store.
Why It Fails. Errors, secrets, stale facts, and injected instructions persist.
Recommended Pattern. Separate working/episodic/semantic/profile memory with purpose, scope, TTL, provenance,
verification, deletion.
Architecture. Candidate → gate → MemoryRecord → scoped retrieval → revalidation.
Enterprise Example. Store a verified communication preference, not inferred sensitive identity.
Detection Metrics. Write acceptance, stale retrieval, corrections, cross-scope access, memory failures.
Trade-offs. Strict eligibility reduces personalization.

Tools
P-05 — Proposal–authorize–commit tool façade
Problem. An agent must cause a business effect.
Anti-Pattern. Direct broad CRUD call with ambient credentials.
Why It Fails. Language becomes authorization and retries duplicate work.
Recommended Pattern. Typed tool, least privilege, policy, proposal/grant, idempotency, reconciliation.
Architecture. Proposal → policy/approval → façade → effect → receipt → acceptance.
Enterprise Example. Payment proposal binds invoice, amount, account, policy, expiry, version.
Detection Metrics. Denials, invalid args, grant mismatch, duplicates, unknown effects.
Trade-offs. Controls add latency; automate low-risk checks.

Multi-agent collaboration
P-06 — Typed task ownership and join
Problem. Specialists collaborate under one outcome.


                                                    BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 86
                                                            ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE



Anti-Pattern. Free-form agent chat owns work.
Why It Fails. Duplicate owners, cycles, lost artifacts, premature completion.
Recommended Pattern. TaskEnvelope, one owner, authority, deadline, artifacts, cancellation, deterministic join.
Architecture. Supervisor → task service → specialists → accepted artifacts → join.
Enterprise Example. Finance, legal, and security deliver required due-diligence artifacts.
Detection Metrics. Orphans, owner conflicts, fan-out, join age, late effects.
Trade-offs. Formal tasks reduce spontaneity.

Security
P-07 — Least-privilege capability envelope
Problem. Agents need sensitive access.
Anti-Pattern. One reusable token follows the chain.
Why It Fails. Injection or compromise gains broad authority.
Recommended Pattern. Subject+workload identity and short-lived audience/purpose/resource/spend/time scope.
Architecture. Identity → policy → capability lease → gateway/tool enforcement.
Enterprise Example. Procurement may read supplier and propose screening, not approve payment.
Detection Metrics. Scope/age, expansion, cross-tenant attempts, denials.
Trade-offs. Fine-grained policy needs templates/simulation.

Reliability
P-08 — Effect ledger and reconciliation
Problem. Timeout may follow a committed external effect.
Anti-Pattern. Timeout means failure and retry.
Why It Fails. Logical effect can execute twice.
Recommended Pattern. Prepared EffectRecord, stable key, unknown terminal, authoritative reconciliation.
Architecture. Prepare → dispatch → receipt/unknown → reconcile → accept.
Enterprise Example. Vendor creation timeout is queried by idempotency key before retry.
Detection Metrics. Unknown rate/age, duplicate effects, reconcile latency.
Trade-offs. Requires downstream receipts/query.

Observability
P-09 — Causal run graph
Problem. Diagnosis spans model, context, policy, tools, humans, effects.
Anti-Pattern. Independent logs and transcripts.
Why It Fails. First divergence and outcome/cost attribution are lost.
Recommended Pattern. Correlated graph linking request, release, context, decisions, effects, acceptance, outcome.
Architecture. Trace IDs + domain IDs → spans/events → evidence/evaluation join.
Enterprise Example. Trace reveals stale retrieval before a correct but misinformed tool call.
Detection Metrics. Unlinked spans, missing versions, outcome join, diagnosis time.
Trade-offs. Capture is costly/sensitive; sample and reference payloads.




                                                  BOOK v1.0.0   |   PAGE 87
                                                               ENTERPRISE AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE & DEVELOPER PLATFORM GUIDE




Governance
P-10 — Risk-tiered evidence gates
Problem. Behavior changes often but review is finite.
Anti-Pattern. Manual-review everything or auto-deploy from one score.
Why It Fails. Fatigue or self-certification hides risk.
Recommended Pattern. Classify risk and require proportional tests, reviewers, canary, rollback.
Architecture. ChangeBundle → risk → validation → approval → staged release.
Enterprise Example. Typo auto-promotes; hiring memory/model changes require domain/privacy/risk approval.
Detection Metrics. Bypass, fatigue, exception age, regression escape.
Trade-offs. Risk classification needs calibration.

Operations
P-11 — Durable runtime with backpressure
Problem. Runs face bursts, waits, humans, unreliable dependencies.
Anti-Pattern. Synchronous workers retry locally.
Why It Fails. State is lost and outages amplify queues/retries.
Recommended Pattern. Durable state, leases, timers, bounded retries, permits, quotas, circuits, degraded mode.
Architecture. Ingress → admission → durable queue/workflow → bounded workers → bulkheads.
Enterprise Example. During registry degradation, protected writes pause and queues stay bounded.
Detection Metrics. Queue age, permits, retry amplification, saturation, recovery.
Trade-offs. Durability adds operational complexity.

System architecture
P-12 — Portable contracts and adapters
Problem. Multiple frameworks/providers must coexist.
Anti-Pattern. App code depends on provider-native objects.
Why It Fails. Migration rewrites apps and controls diverge.
Recommended Pattern. Platform-owned contracts and normalized events with conformance-tested adapters.
Architecture. Application contracts → adapters → shared services → providers/protocols.
Enterprise Example. Framework migration preserves tools, policy, traces, evaluation, release.
Detection Metrics. Native leakage, conformance failures, migration effort.
Trade-offs. Abstraction may lag novel features; allow negotiated extensions.

Transition to Chapter 17
Pattern selection is easier when failure shapes are visible; Chapter 17 recasts recurring anti-patterns as measurable
replacement decisions.
